ERNIE-Image                         *sglang-diffusion-Ernie-Image-Ernie-Image*

Deploy ERNIE-Image and ERNIE-Image-Turbo with SGLang Diffusion.

1. QUICK START               *diffusion-Ernie-Image-Ernie-Image-1-quick-start*

Follow the |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation| for your GPU
platform.

2. MODEL CAPABILITIES *diffusion-Ernie-Image-Ernie-Image-2-model-capabilities*

ERNIE-Image https://huggingface.co/baidu/ERNIE-Image is Baidu's
text-to-image family with separate standard and Turbo checkpoints. The
standard model is the quality-oriented path; Turbo is the lower-latency
choice, and both load through SGLang's native `ErnieImagePipeline`.

This integration targets text-only image generation. You can use a single
GPU or Ulysses sequence parallelism; use a dedicated editing model when
preserving source-image structure is part of the task.

Model              Hugging Face model ID    Notes
-----------------  -----------------------  ----------------------------------
ERNIE-Image        baidu/ERNIE-Image        Regular text-to-image checkpoint
ERNIE-Image-Turbo  baidu/ERNIE-Image-Turbo  Turbo text-to-image checkpoint

3. SERVE THE MODEL       *diffusion-Ernie-Image-Ernie-Image-3-serve-the-model*

The commands below target a single supported NVIDIA CUDA or AMD ROCm GPU.
Start with `--performance-mode auto`; use `speed` only when the full
pipeline fits comfortably on the selected GPU(s), and use `memory` when you
need lower peak GPU memory.

Serve ERNIE-Image:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path baidu/ERNIE-Image \
      --num-gpus 1 \
      --performance-mode auto \
      --port 30010
<
Serve ERNIE-Image-Turbo:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path baidu/ERNIE-Image-Turbo \
      --num-gpus 1 \
      --performance-mode auto \
      --port 30010
<
Intel Xeon CPU deployment is supported by prefixing the server command with
`SGLANG_DIFFUSION_PLATFORM_OVERRIDE=cpu` and omitting GPU-only flags.

Serve ERNIE-Image:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    SGLANG_DIFFUSION_PLATFORM_OVERRIDE=cpu sglang serve --model-path baidu/ERNIE-Image --port 30010
<
Serve ERNIE-Image-Turbo:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    SGLANG_DIFFUSION_PLATFORM_OVERRIDE=cpu sglang serve --model-path baidu/ERNIE-Image-Turbo --port 30010
<

4. GENERATE AN IMAGE   *diffusion-Ernie-Image-Ernie-Image-4-generate-an-image*

Use the OpenAI-compatible image generation API after the server starts:

[Python]

[Python]Python

>python
    import base64
    from openai import OpenAI

    client = OpenAI(api_key="EMPTY", base_url="http://127.0.0.1:30010/v1")

    response = client.images.generate(
        model="baidu/ERNIE-Image-Turbo",
        prompt="A cinematic photo of a quiet lakeside cabin at sunrise",
        n=1,
        response_format="b64_json",
    )

    image_bytes = base64.b64decode(response.data[0].b64_json)
    with open("ernie_image.png", "wb") as f:
        f.write(image_bytes)
<

5. CONFIGURATION TIPS *diffusion-Ernie-Image-Ernie-Image-5-configuration-tips*

- ERNIE-Image is a text-to-image pipeline; do not pass `--image-path`.
- `--performance-mode auto` keeps conservative defaults while preserving
  explicit user flags.
- If the checkpoint includes a PE component, SGLang loads it automatically
  with the native Ministral3 runtime. Use
  `--layerwise-offload-components pe` when the local PE decoder needs to
  trade latency for lower GPU memory usage.
- On NVIDIA CUDA, use
  `--num-gpus 2 --tp-size 1 --ulysses-degree 2 --encoder-parallel replicate`
  to shard the DiT image tokens across two GPUs while retaining the full
  text on each rank. Both checkpoints use this path. Measure your target
  resolution: communication can make smaller images slower.
- Ulysses sharding requires an evenly divisible image-token count and
  unmasked text conditioning. Uneven image-token counts, padded caption
  batches with a mask, and ring configurations retain dense DiT execution on
  each rank. Ring does not accelerate this model.
- FSDP, TP, caching, and breakable CUDA graphs need separate validation; the
  Ulysses path does not establish compatibility with these features.

6. RUN IN COMFYUI         *diffusion-Ernie-Image-Ernie-Image-6-run-in-comfyui*

----------------------------------------------------------------------------
FLUX.3                                          *sglang-diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3*

Serve FLUX.3 Image Klein text-to-image generation and image editing with
SGLang Diffusion.

1. QUICK START                           *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-1-quick-start*

Use a |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation|. Until a released image
contains this integration, install SGLang from `main` inside it:

>bash
    pip install 'sglang[diffusion] @ git+https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang.git#subdirectory=python'
<
  Note: ~

  The picker selects `black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-base` or
  `black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein`, each a self-contained repository.
  Access to the selected checkpoint is required. Keep `--model-path` at its
  repository root; `--model-variant` selects only the quantized DiT. Both
  release BF16 checkpoints have single-GPU generation checks; see the
  validation boundaries below. Unverified hardware or resource shapes remain
  selectable but are not measured recommendations.

The picker defaults to resident weights for its 1 MP, batch-1 workload. Its
recommended execution uses BCG only where a matched E2E comparison showed a
material gain: currently single-GB300 distilled NVFP4 T2I. Other
combinations start eager; this graph recipe covers the 80-token text bucket.
Changing precision never happens automatically. Recommendations are best
among the tested settings, not an exhaustive optimum.

  Warning: ~

  FLUX.3 Image Klein is released under the FLUX Kommunity License v1.0
  https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein/blob/main/LICENSE.md:
  non-commercial use, plus commercial output generation for Qualifying Users
  with less than US$5M in gross annualized revenue, subject to its terms.
  The Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct text encoder is Apache-2.0. Review the license
  before production or commercial use.

For platform-specific installation, see the
|sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation|.

2. MODEL CAPABILITIES             *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-2-model-capabilities*

FLUX.3 Image Klein
https://huggingface.co/collections/black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein is
Black Forest Labs' 5.6B open-weights model for text-to-image generation and
image editing. It takes a prompt and up to 10 reference images and returns
one image; text-to-image, single-reference edits and multi-reference
compositions run through the same DiT, with Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct as the text
encoder. The 5.6B count covers the image transformer only.

Prompts can place elements with bounding boxes on a 0 to 1000 grid, then
recolor, move, replace or remove them; boxes guide placement and scale
rather than masking pixels. The model also renders text inside images. One
SGLang server serves text-to-image and editing, chosen per request. This
image pipeline does not serve the separate FLUX.3 Action robot policy.

Checkpoint  Training                 Steps  Guidance  Built for
----------  -----------------------  -----  --------  ------------------------
Klein       step-distilled flow map  16     none      generation and editing,
                                                      self-hosting; the
                                                      checkpoint behind the
                                                      BFL API
Klein Base  undistilled              50     3.5       fine-tuning, LoRA
                                                      training, research

Each repository ships its BF16 DiT with FP8 (`fp8r`) and NVFP4 variants,
together with the text encoder and image autoencoder. The default
|docs-sglang-diffusion-caching-acceleration-conditioning-cache| reuses
prompt encodings, including the Base model's negative prompt. It does not
skip denoising steps or change the checkpoint. Cache-DiT, described below,
is a separate approximation.

Task            Endpoint                     Conditioning
--------------  ---------------------------  ---------------------------------
Text to image   POST /v1/images/generations  Text prompt
Image to image  POST /v1/images/edits        Text prompt and 1 to 10 image
                                             uploads

Start the server:

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-base \
      --host 0.0.0.0 \
      --port 30010
<
Sampling is set per request (`num_inference_steps`, `guidance_scale`,
`size`, `seed`, `n`); unset fields take the checkpoint's defaults from its
`config.json`. SGLang expands `n` into independently seeded requests and can
pack compatible requests into one DiT forward.

References are prepared as in the BFL reference pipeline
https://github.com/black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein. A single reference
is fitted under 4 MP, reflect-padded to the 16-pixel grid and, when you set
no output size, sets the output size; the padding is cropped from the
result. With several references each is fitted under 1 MP and
centre-cropped, and the output takes the first one's size.

Prompting                                    *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-prompting*

The caption controls composition; reference images anchor content but do not
lock structure.

- Write full scene captions covering framing, subject, background and
  lighting. A short prompt gives generic output, and a one-line edit
  instruction (“make it blue”) reframes the shot. The
  |diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-7-prompt-upsampler| expands a short request into a
  full caption.
- For edits, describe the target image as a complete caption of the
  reference with the edit folded in.
- For layout, append a JSON list of elements to the caption. Each box is
  `[top, left, bottom, right]` on a 0 to 1000 grid, and the caption tags
  each element as `<id>`. To edit by box, refer to the n-th reference as
  `<ref_image_n>` and give each element `from`, `src_bbox` and `tgt_bbox`; a
  `null` target removes it. See BFL's bounding box guide
  https://docs.bfl.ai/flux_3/flux3_image_bounding_boxes for the full format.

                           *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-3-checkpoints-and-precision*
3. CHECKPOINTS AND PRECISION

Repository                   --model-variant      Sampling
---------------------------  -------------------  ----------------------------
black-forest-labs/flux-3-    Omit / fp8r / nvfp4  Base CFG; defaults from the
image-klein-base                                  selected configuration
black-forest-labs/flux-3-    Omit / fp8r / nvfp4  Step-distilled flow map; no
image-klein                                       CFG

Both repositories use `flux-3-image-klein` in configuration and safetensors
metadata. This shared identifier does not determine the sampling mode. The
release repositories do not need the pre-release `sd-*` variant names.

The distilled model is a step-distilled flow-map DiT: each step jumps
straight to the next time of the schedule, so it samples without CFG. SGLang
tells a flow map from its weights. It rejects `--enable-cfg-parallel`, a
request `guidance_scale` other than 1, and a non-empty `negative_prompt`.
Tensor parallelism, Ulysses SP and breakable CUDA graphs work as for the
base model.

The Base model defaults to CFG 3.5 against an empty negative prompt. Both
variants reuse the package's text encoder and VAE. Keep the checkpoint
revision, precision, attention backend, guidance and sampling parameters
fixed when comparing outputs. Explicit `guidance_scale=1` uses scale 1, not
the package default; `true_cfg_scale`, when provided, takes precedence over
`guidance_scale`.

Quantized checkpoints            *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-quantized-checkpoints*

Each repo ships quantized DiTs under `variants/`: select one with
`--model-variant fp8r` or `--model-variant nvfp4` and keep `--model-path`
pointing at the repo root. Text encoder and VAE precision follow their own
package settings; quantizing the DiT does not quantize them. An explicit
`--component-precisions.vae bf16` overrides the package dtype for both
reference-image encoding and output decoding. The quantization comes from
the variant's `hf_quant_config.json`, so do not also pass `--quantization`.

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-base \
      --model-variant fp8r \
      --host 0.0.0.0 \
      --port 30010
<
FP8R stores rowwise-FP8 weights. On Blackwell (SM10x) the FP8 projections
run FlashInfer PrimsTS GEMMs with fused QK-norm/RoPE and SwiGLU epilogues
(FlashInfer >= 0.7.1); set `SGLANG_ENABLE_FLUX3_PRIMS_TS_AUTOTUNE=1` to
autotune them during warmup. Other GPUs use the generic rowwise-FP8 GEMM.

NVFP4 stores FP4 (E2M1) weights with FP8 block scales and needs a Blackwell
GPU and a compatible GEMM implementation. SM10x and consumer SM120 use
different kernel paths; a B200 recipe does not establish RTX 5090
performance. Both quantization modes can change output quality and need
workload-specific comparison against BF16.

These flags remain separately configurable; see the validation boundaries
below before combining quantization with other optimizations.

Breakable CUDA graphs            *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-breakable-cuda-graphs*

BCG reserves additional device memory and captures specific request shapes.
The picker generates `--warmup-resolutions 1024x1024` and matching
`--warmup-sampling-params` for the selected quality. For editing it also
sets `flux3_mode="i2i"` and a server-local reference path: mount a 1024x1024
image there before starting the server. This path can differ from the
client's upload path. Longer prompt token buckets, other reference
dimensions/counts, batches or quality settings may run eager instead; check
for a serving-signature miss in the server log.

Changing the picker workload also changes its graph warmup command. Restart
with that command to capture the new workload. Keep eager for variable-shape
traffic, or explicitly validate every shape you intend to capture.

LoRA adapters                            *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-lora-adapters*

Use the standard LoRA API with native DiT keys, optionally prefixed by
`dit.` or `transformer.`. BF16 dynamic and merged adapters have a synthetic
rank-4 generation check, including repeatability and exact restoration after
unloading; this does not validate every community adapter. For quantized
deployments requiring LoRA, pass `--lora-path` at startup to select the
generic quantized layout. Attaching LoRA after PrimsTS weight preparation is
rejected before replacing any layers. FP8R and NVFP4 startup adapters also
have synthetic rank-4 generation checks on GB300, including repeatability
and exact restoration after unloading. These do not establish compatibility
with arbitrary quantized community adapters.

4. MULTI-GPU AND MEMORY         *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-4-multi-gpu-and-memory*

FLUX.3 shares its DiT with FLUX.3 Action, so the image pipeline supports
tensor parallelism, Ulysses sequence parallelism, CFG parallelism, layerwise
offload and breakable CUDA graphs. The picker covers RTX 5090, RTX PRO 6000,
H100/H200, and B200/B300/GB200/GB300 with one to eight GPUs on one node. TP
times Ulysses degree must equal the GPU count and evenly partition the 24
attention heads. Ring is not admitted by this image recipe.

For example, two-GPU TP uses `--num-gpus 2 --tp-size 2`; two-GPU Ulysses
uses `--num-gpus 2 --tp-size 1 --ulysses-degree 2`. Base also permits CFG
parallelism; distilled checkpoints have no CFG branch to parallelize. More
GPUs do not guarantee lower latency, particularly over PCIe.

Ulysses inherits all-to-all streaming through the shared attention layer.
Leave its pipeline-group policy automatic; short sequences need not benefit.
TP-only deployments do not use this path. Encoding-while-decoding overlaps
video-frame decoding with video-file encoding, so it does not apply to this
still-image pipeline.

  Note: ~

  Under sequence parallelism every rank holds the full DiT, so combining it
  with layerwise offload streams the whole model to each GPU every step. TP
  also partitions weights, so it is the lower-memory starting point when
  offloading on several GPUs. Preserve headroom for the longest prompt,
  references and output size you intend to serve; a successful small request
  is not a capacity guarantee.

The VAE decoder runs unsharded by default even with multiple GPUs.
`--vae-sp true` splits decoding along the image height when a
decode-parallel group is available; it does not shard reference-image
encoding.

For FP32 VAE attention above 1 MP, `quality="lossless"` can use
dense-layout, full-context memory-efficient SDPA; `quality="exact"` retains
the original path. It does not prune attention or reduce precision, but
reduction order can change pixels. In a Base BF16 test with a 2048x2048
reference and 1024x1024 output, GB300 peak allocated memory fell from 54.3
to 29.5 GiB; latency was effectively unchanged. GB300/H200 image SSIM was
0.9951-0.9992 against the exact path across four samples. This is allocated
memory, not total device usage: a 32 GB card still needs offload or
quantization headroom for that reference size.

Disaggregated serving            *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-disaggregated-serving*

You can deploy the prompt encoder, reference-image VAE encoder, DiT, and VAE
decoder as separate worker pools behind the same image API. Both T2I and i2i
are supported. Use the
|docs-sglang-diffusion-disaggregation-flux3-split-encoding| for the
four-role commands, or keep the existing three-role topology with a combined
encoder. This separates resources and scaling; it adds transfer hops and
does not imply lower single-request latency. T2I skips the image encoder;
editing keeps reference padding/crop semantics. Each pool can have a
different number of workers. Keep the package variant and numerical settings
consistent across workers; the guide includes the required transfer
dependency, network ports, and same-node validation scope.

5. CACHE-DIT                               *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-5-cache-dit*

|sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-cache_dit| skips the 28 single-stream blocks
on steps where their input barely changes and reuses the cached residual.
Turn it on for every request with `SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_ENABLED=true` on
`sglang serve`, or per request with `"enable_cache_dit": true`. A request
can also tune it, for example
`"cache_dit_params": {"residual_diff_threshold": 0.12, "enable_taylorseer": true}`.

For image editing, send `-F "enable_cache_dit=true"` and a JSON-encoded
`cache_dit_params` form field; generation uses JSON request fields.

Cache-DiT is approximate and may change composition; measure quality and
latency on representative prompts rather than treating its threshold as a
fidelity guarantee. It combines with FP8R and NVFP4, tensor parallelism, CFG
parallelism and layerwise offload. It is skipped, with a warning, under
Ulysses sequence parallelism and breakable CUDA graphs, and for a request
whose CFG pair has to run as two forwards to fit in device memory.

6. REQUEST EXAMPLES                 *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-6-request-examples*

Text to image                            *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-text-to-image*

>bash
    curl -sS -X POST http://localhost:30010/v1/images/generations \
      -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
      -d '{"prompt": "A red fox standing in fresh snow", "size": "1024x1024", "seed": 0, "response_format": "b64_json"}' \
      -o flux3_t2i.json
<

Image to image                          *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-image-to-image*

>bash
    curl -sS -X POST http://localhost:30010/v1/images/edits \
      -F "image=@/data/flux3/reference.png" \
      -F "prompt=Render the reference as a winter wildlife photograph" \
      -F "seed=0" \
      -F "response_format=b64_json" \
      -o flux3_i2i.json
<

Offline generation                  *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-offline-generation*

>bash
    sglang generate \
      --model-path black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-base \
      --height 1024 --width 1024 \
      --num-inference-steps 50 \
      --guidance-scale 3.5 \
      --prompt "A red fox standing in fresh snow"
<
Pass `--image-path` (one or more images) to edit references instead.

7. PROMPT UPSAMPLER                 *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-7-prompt-upsampler*

black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-pup
https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-pup is a
Qwen3.5-9B vision-language fine-tune that expands a short prompt into the
detailed caption FLUX.3 works best with. The BFL reference code runs it in
front of the image model by default. It is a standard
`Qwen3_5ForConditionalGeneration` checkpoint, so it is served as a regular
LLM, separately from the image pipeline. The system prompt
(`system_prompts.json` in the repository) selects the mode; `t2i` is the
text-to-image upsampler.

Download the package and start the upsampler on its own port:

>bash
    PUP_PATH="<local-path>"
    hf download black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-pup --local-dir ${PUP_PATH}

    CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1 sglang serve ${PUP_PATH} \
          --model-type llm \
          --served-model-name flux-3-image-klein-pup \
          --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8001 \
          --context-length 16384 \
          --mem-fraction-static 0.90 \
          --reasoning-parser qwen3
<
Upsample a text prompt through the OpenAI-compatible chat endpoint. The
`t2i` system prompt is read from the downloaded package; thinking is
disabled and decoding follows the model card (greedy, presence penalty 1.5):

>bash
    jq -n --slurpfile p ${PUP_PATH}/system_prompts.json '{
        model: "flux-3-image-klein-pup",
        messages: [
          {role: "system", content: $p[0].t2i},
          {role: "user",   content: "A man walking with his dog, afternoon sun"}
        ],
        max_tokens: 3584,
        temperature: 0,
        presence_penalty: 1.5,
        chat_template_kwargs: {enable_thinking: false}
      }' | curl -s http://0.0.0.0:8001/v1/chat/completions \
             -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d @- \
         | jq -r '.choices[0].message.content'
<
You can then pass the returned caption as the `--prompt` of a `t2i` request
to the Flux3 pipeline.

To have the image server call the upsampler itself, point it at this
endpoint with the built-in `flux3-klein-pup` protocol. It reads
`system_prompts.json` from the upsampler package (`protocol_source`, a local
directory or the Hugging Face repo, which is the default) and follows BFL's
reference code: the `t2i` prompt for generations, the `i2i` prompt with the
references first (resized as the reference code does) for edits, greedy
decoding with presence penalty 1.5 for `t2i` and 0 for `i2i`, and thinking
disabled.

>bash
    cat > flux3_pup.json <<'JSON'
    {
      "base_url": "http://127.0.0.1:8001/v1",
      "model": "flux-3-image-klein-pup",
      "protocol": "flux3-klein-pup",
      "protocol_source": "<local-path>"
    }
    JSON

    sglang serve --model-path black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-base \
      --prompt-enhancer-config flux3_pup.json --port 30010
<
A request opts in with `enhance_prompt`; it is off by default, so a full
caption is sent as typed:

>bash
    curl -sS -X POST http://localhost:30010/v1/images/generations \
      -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
      -d '{"prompt": "A man walking with his dog, afternoon sun", "enhance_prompt": true, "size": "1024x1024", "response_format": "b64_json"}'
<
Greedy decoding is not by itself reproducible here: the caption changes with
the upsampler's prefix cache and batch. Start the upsampler with
`--enable-deterministic-inference --disable-radix-cache` when the same
request must give the same caption.

The layout prompts (`t2i_bbox`, `i2i_bbox`) are not wired in yet; write the
box table into the prompt yourself as described in
|diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-2-model-capabilities|.

8. DYNAMIC BATCHING                 *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-8-dynamic-batching*

Enable a small batching window to collect concurrent requests:

[Serve concurrent requests]

[Serve concurrent requests]Serve concurrent requests

>bash
    sglang serve --model-path black-forest-labs/flux-3-image-klein-base \
      --batching-max-size 2 --batching-delay-ms 5
<
The existing scheduler groups requests with matching sampling/runtime
settings. Each keeps its own prompt, seed and reference images. Text
encoding groups unique prompts by token-length bucket; DiT attention remains
isolated per packed sample, including each CFG branch. VAE decoding groups
outputs by canvas size before restoring request order and each reference
crop.

Larger batches need more activation memory and may increase individual
request latency. Start with batch 2, compare throughput and tail latency
under your actual QPS, and keep batch 1 for low-latency or low-VRAM
workloads. Batching does not imply bitwise equality with separate forwards:
GEMM and attention reduction choices can change with shape. Check output
fidelity with fixed prompts and seeds.

                     *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-pre-release-batching-measurements*
Pre-release batching measurements

These measurements used pre-release weights, not the release repositories
above. They do not establish release-checkpoint performance.

The following are warmed HTTP measurements on one GB300 (about 277 GiB
device memory), Base BF16, Torch SDPA, eager execution and all components
resident. Each row compares two different prompt/seed requests served
serially with the same two requests served together. Times include local
HTTP transfer and JPEG output; medians cover three rounds after warming both
shapes and reference encoding. Software: PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130, Transformers
5.19.0. Checkpoint revision: `5de4fe32f17aee3dbc34375a94935f09206dba2c`.

Workload                       Serial pair  Batch-2 pair    Throughput ratio
-----------------------------  -----------  --------------  ------------------
T2I, 512x512, 8 steps          0.649 s      0.324 s         2.00x
I2I, 512x512, 8 steps          0.737 s      0.546 s         1.35x
T2I, 1024x1024, 50 steps       6.248 s      6.050 s         1.03x
I2I, 1024x1024, 50 steps       13.171 s     12.996 s        1.01x

Eight steps exercise batching, not the recommended Base image-quality
setting. The standard 50-step workload shows little batching benefit;
FlashAttention gives a similarly small gain in this setup. Batch 1 remains
the default. Do not extrapolate the small-request speedup to full-resolution
generation or other GPUs.

For these 50-step requests, batch-1 versus batch-2 JPEG outputs had SSIM
0.9920-0.9977. A separate four-image PNG comparison at 512x512/8 steps had
SSIM 0.9885-0.9955; JPEG can amplify small rounding differences. These are
observed samples, not a universal quality guarantee. Identical single
requests before and after batching matched exactly. Preserve additional
memory headroom for batching rather than assuming a batch-1 recipe fits on a
32 GB card at batch 2.

9. VALIDATION BOUNDARIES       *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-9-validation-boundaries*

The release BF16 and FP8R checkpoints have single-GB300/H200 generation
checks; NVFP4 has GB300 and RTX 5090 checks. RTX 5090 also has Base BF16 and
both checkpoints' FP8R and Cache-DiT checks. Single-GPU measurements use
eager execution, all components resident, batch 1, 1024x1024 T2I and I2I,
two prompts/seeds and repeated requests. Base uses 50 steps and distilled
uses 16. Repeated requests matched pixel-for-pixel within each tested
configuration. This does not establish official-reference parity or validate
every combination of hardware, parallelism, batching and optional features
in the picker.

Multi-GPU measurements          *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-multi-gpu-measurements*

Base BF16, 50 steps, CFG 3.5, batch 1 and `quality="exact"`. These are
warmed localhost HTTP medians through PNG decoding, excluding loading and
warmup; two prompts/seeds and two timed rounds per prompt. Software: PyTorch
2.14.1+cu130, Transformers 5.19.0, FlashInfer 0.7.1. Both runs use
checkpoint `d8acde427a698bdb98b5c1c941c60fa4c97e9f28`. The H200 run uses FA3
and NVLink; the RTX 4090 run uses Torch SDPA over PCIe without CUDA peer
access.

GPUs         Topology                  T2I 1024x1024  I2I, 1 MP
                                                      reference/output
-----------  ------------------------  -------------  ------------------------
2x H200      TP2                       4.27 s         Not measured
2x H200      Ulysses 2, streaming      4.68 s         Not measured
             auto
4x RTX 4090  TP4                       16.97 s        32.61 s
4x RTX 4090  TP2 x Ulysses 2           16.28 s        32.63 s

The picker keeps TP as its multi-GPU starting point, not a guarantee that TP
always wins. A hybrid topology can help on PCIe; validate it on your own
interconnect. H100, B200 and other unmeasured resource shapes are
extrapolated starting points and are not labelled verified.

On the same two H200s, Ulysses streaming reduced 2048x2048 T2I from 24.00 to
22.87 s and editing a 2048x2048 reference into a 1024x1024 output from 31.01
to 29.54 s. Streamed and non-streamed outputs matched pixel-for-pixel in
these samples, with no quantization or caching. At 1024x1024, automatic
dispatch selected no streaming groups and gave no speedup. TP and SP
themselves are not bit-identical: sample SSIM was 0.9890-0.9985. None of
these comparisons establishes which path is closer to an official reference.

Single-GPU measurements        *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-single-gpu-measurements*

Warmed local HTTP medians, including PNG encoding and response transfer,
from two rounds per prompt. PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130, Transformers 5.19.0, FP32
VAE, `quality="exact"`; FlashAttention on GB300/H200 and Torch SDPA on RTX
5090. The Base checkpoint revision is
`d8acde427a698bdb98b5c1c941c60fa4c97e9f28`. SSIM compares each quantized
result with BF16 on the same GPU, prompt, seed and reference, and reports
the lowest value across two samples, not a quality bound.

GPU                 Base DiT  T2I      I2I      Minimum SSIM, T2I / I2I
------------------  --------  -------  -------  ------------------------------
GB300, 277 GiB      BF16      3.21 s   6.31 s   Reference
GB300, 277 GiB      FP8R      2.18 s   4.49 s   0.958 / 0.981
GB300, 277 GiB      NVFP4     2.14 s   4.31 s   0.919 / 0.958
H200, 140 GiB       BF16      7.09 s   14.52 s  Reference
H200, 140 GiB       FP8R      5.15 s   11.24 s  0.955 / 0.984
RTX 5090, 32 GB     BF16      20.39 s  43.4 s   Reference

For the distilled checkpoint, quantization's T2I benefit was small on GB300;
do not assume Base's speedup transfers to a 16-step workload. Cache-DiT at
threshold 0.12 reduced Base I2I from 6.31 to 3.08 s on GB300, with sample
SSIM 0.9786-0.9975. It remains opt-in and approximate.

Eager versus CUDA graphs      *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-eager-versus-cuda-graphs*

Matched ABBA comparisons use `quality="lossless"`, resident weights, batch
1, 1024x1024 output/reference, and the same software versions above with
stable FlashInfer 0.7.1. Base uses 50 steps/CFG 3.5; distilled uses 16
steps/no CFG. Times are warmed localhost HTTP medians through JPEG decoding:
two prompts/seeds and four timed samples per arm, excluding loading and
graph capture. These JPEG timings are not directly comparable with the
earlier PNG table.

GPU           Checkpoint             Workload  Eager     BCG       Recommended
------------  ---------------------  --------  --------  --------  -----------
GB300         Distilled NVFP4, FA4   T2I       0.563 s   0.436 s   BCG
GB300         Distilled NVFP4, FA4   I2I       0.882 s   0.886 s   Eager
GB300         Base BF16, FA4         T2I       3.103 s   3.130 s   Eager
GB300         Base BF16, FA4         I2I       6.236 s   6.733 s   Eager
H200          Base BF16, FA3         T2I       6.845 s   6.916 s   Eager
H200          Base BF16, FA3         I2I       14.183 s  15.252 s  Eager
2x H200, TP2  Base BF16, FA3         T2I       4.000 s   4.199 s   Eager
RTX 5090      Distilled NVFP4, SDPA  T2I       1.676 s   1.678 s   Eager
RTX 5090      Distilled NVFP4, SDPA  I2I       4.092 s   4.104 s   Eager
RTX 5090      Base NVFP4, SDPA       T2I       9.449 s   9.583 s   Eager

GB300 distilled NVFP4 T2I improves by 22.5% with FA4 + BCG. Peak allocated
memory increases from 16.63 to 17.46 GiB; peak reserved rises from 18.75 to
19.96 GiB. These are per-process allocator maxima across warmup/runtime, not
loading or total-device peaks. Other workloads also need headroom; memory
capacity alone is not a reason to enable graphs when they do not improve
latency.

All 15 fixed-backend eager/BCG workload pairs passed repeated-request checks
and pixel-identical PNG comparisons across the two prompts. This is not an
official-reference accuracy guarantee or evidence that quantization
preserves BF16 output. GB300 measurements use Base revision
`17333191c36bae1b61b0a247e33ba284f1df07fa` and distilled
`875076bf83d4364b68dccdbf8307f4f01475f68d`; H200/5090 use
`d8acde427a698bdb98b5c1c941c60fa4c97e9f28` and
`4d02a6a4dc4d5c7a0f3fa767187483b2b70f2384`. TP2 uses tensor-identical Base
DiT weights with a different safetensors header.

GB300 distilled NVFP4 T2I also ran at 0.424 s with SDPA + BCG (versus 0.589
s SDPA eager). It is selectable but not the default backend: SDPA versus FA4
outputs had sample SSIM 0.978-0.987 and MAD 1.74-1.75/255. Neither path is
established as closer to the official output. Keep the backend fixed when
evaluating the graph speedup.

The native DiT's packed CFG forward has focused comparison tests against the
shared FLUX 3 transformer. Such component tests do not replace
full-checkpoint E2E or establish parity with every upstream release. Keep
unverified hardware, topologies and optional optimizations separate from
measured deployment recipes.

The opt-in full-checkpoint HTTP regression covers concurrent prompts, image
editing, multiple outputs and a repeated single request:

>bash
    SGLANG_TEST_FLUX3_IMAGE=1 pytest -q \
      python/sglang/multimodal_gen/test/server/test_server_flux3_image.py
<
Set `SGLANG_TEST_FLUX3_IMAGE_MODEL` to a local package path when using an
offline checkpoint. This test needs checkpoint access; it does not
substitute for an official-reference golden-image comparison.

10. COMFYUI                                 *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX.3-10-comfyui*

----------------------------------------------------------------------------
FLUX                                              *sglang-diffusion-FLUX-FLUX*

Deploy FLUX diffusion models with SGLang - 12B/32B rectified flow
transformers for high-quality text-to-image generation.

1. QUICK START                             *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-1-quick-start*

Follow the |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation| for your GPU
platform.

2. MODEL CAPABILITIES               *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-2-model-capabilities*

FLUX https://blackforestlabs.ai/ is Black Forest Labs' rectified-flow image
model family. FLUX.1-dev https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-dev
is the smaller 12B text-to-image checkpoint; FLUX.2-dev
https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-dev is a 32B model that adds
instruction-based editing plus single- and multi-reference composition.

FLUX is a strong default when prompt adherence, polished image quality, or
reference consistency matters. The tradeoff is deployment weight: FLUX.2
needs substantially more memory than FLUX.1, and the dev checkpoints use the
FLUX non-commercial license, so review the model license before production
use.

Checkpoint                Best fit                   Main limitation
------------------------  -------------------------  -------------------------
black-forest-labs/FLUX.   High-quality               No native multi-reference
1-dev                     text-to-image with the     editing path
                          lighter FLUX deployment
black-forest-labs/FLUX.   Text-to-image, editing,    32B model with a larger
2-dev                     and reference-guided       memory footprint
                          composition in one model

3. MODEL DEPLOYMENT                   *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-3-model-deployment*

This section provides deployment configurations optimized for different
hardware platforms and use cases.

3.1 Basic Configuration          *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-3-1-basic-configuration*

FLUX models are optimized for high-quality image generation. The recommended
launch configurations vary by hardware and model version.

Use the |diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-1-quick-start| above.

3.2 Configuration Tips            *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-3-2-configuration-tips*

See |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-performance-optimization| for acceleration
features and their runtime requirements.

- `--vae-path`: Path to a custom VAE model or HuggingFace model ID (e.g.,
  fal/FLUX.2-Tiny-AutoEncoder). If not specified, the VAE will be loaded
  from the main model path.
- `--num-gpus`: Number of GPUs to use
- `--tp-size`: Tensor parallelism size; shards the FLUX DiT across GPUs.
  Text encoder offload does not require TP 1; see
  |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-encoder_parallel| for encoder placement and
  sharding.
- `--sp-degree`: Sequence parallelism size (typically should match the
  number of GPUs)
- `--ulysses-degree`: The degree of DeepSpeed-Ulysses-style SP in USP
- `--ring-degree`: The degree of ring attention-style SP in USP

                 *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-3-3-breakable-cuda-graph-for-flux-1-dev*
3.3 Breakable CUDA graph for FLUX.1-dev

For repeated `quality=exact` requests, FLUX.1-dev supports breakable CUDA
graph (BCG) execution with the native backend. BCG captures DiT segments
during startup and replays them for the warmed resolutions. It can reduce
recurring host launch overhead without enabling `torch.compile`.

The following configuration was validated on two NVIDIA H200 GPUs with BF16
weights, PyTorch 2.13, and CUDA 13.0. Set `HF_TOKEN` to a Hugging Face token
with access to the checkpoint before downloading it.

>bash
    CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0,1 sglang generate \
      --model-path black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-dev \
      --backend sglang \
      --num-gpus 2 --tp-size 2 \
      --component-residency dit=resident \
      --enable-torch-compile false \
      --enable-breakable-cuda-graph \
      --warmup-resolutions 1024x1024 \
      --warmup-sampling-params '{"quality": "exact"}' \
      --quality exact \
      --width 1024 --height 1024 \
      --num-inference-steps 50 --guidance-scale 3.5 --seed 42 \
      --prompt "A futuristic cyberpunk city at night, neon lights reflecting on wet streets"
<
Confirm `[Diffusion BCG] captured` in the log, then compare warmed request
latency with eager execution on the same GPUs. Capture time and graph memory
are additional startup costs. Add other served resolutions to
`--warmup-resolutions`; a request with an uncaptured signature falls back to
eager.

FLUX.1-dev uses a fixed 512-token T5 conditioning sequence, so changing
`--bcg-text-buckets` does not create additional prompt-length graphs. BCG
captures graphs only at the quality level of the warmup request, which is
the server's default request level (`lossless`) unless
`--warmup-sampling-params '{"quality": "exact"}'` sets it, as in the command
above. A request at any other level (for example `high`, which mounts
request-gated DiT fusions) has no captured graph and runs eager. FLUX.2 and
quantized transformer overrides require separate validation.

4. API USAGE                                 *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-4-api-usage*

For complete API documentation, please refer to the
|sglang-sglang-diffusion-api-openai_api|.

4.1 Generate an Image              *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-4-1-generate-an-image*

[Example]

[Example]Example

>python
    import base64
    from openai import OpenAI

    client = OpenAI(api_key="EMPTY", base_url="http://localhost:30000/v1")

    response = client.images.generate(
        model="black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-dev",
        prompt="A cat holding a sign that says hello world",
        size="1024x1024",
        n=1,
        response_format="b64_json",
    )

    # Save the generated image
    image_bytes = base64.b64decode(response.data[0].b64_json)
    with open("output.png", "wb") as f:
        f.write(image_bytes)
<

4.2 Advanced Usage                    *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-4-2-advanced-usage*

                            *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-4-2-1-cache-dit-acceleration*
4.2.1 Cache-DiT Acceleration

SGLang integrates Cache-DiT https://github.com/vipshop/cache-dit, a caching
acceleration engine for Diffusion Transformers (DiT), to achieve up to 7.4x
inference speedup with minimal quality loss. You can set
`SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_ENABLED=True` to enable it. For more details, please refer
to the SGLang Cache-DiT |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-cache_dit|.

Basic Usage

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_ENABLED=true sglang serve --model-path black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-dev
<
Advanced Usage

- DBCache Parameters: DBCache controls block-level caching behavior:

Parameter  Env Variable             Default  Description
---------  -----------------------  -------  ---------------------------------
Fn         SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_FN      1        Number of first blocks to always
                                             compute
Bn         SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_BN      0        Number of last blocks to always
                                             compute
W          SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_WARMUP  4        Warmup steps before caching
                                             starts
R          SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_RDT     0.24     Residual difference threshold
MC         SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_MC      3        Maximum continuous cached steps

- TaylorSeer Configuration: TaylorSeer improves caching accuracy using
  Taylor expansion:

Parameter  Env Variable                 Default  Description
---------  ---------------------------  -------  -----------------------------
Enable     SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_TAYLORSEER  false    Enable TaylorSeer calibrator
Order      SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_TS_ORDER    1        Taylor expansion order (1 or
                                                 2)

Combined Configuration Example:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_ENABLED=true \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_FN=2 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_BN=1 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_WARMUP=4 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_RDT=0.4 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_MC=4 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_TAYLORSEER=true \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_TS_ORDER=2 \
    sglang serve --model-path black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-dev
<

4.2.2 CPU Offload                      *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-4-2-2-cpu-offload*

- `--dit-cpu-offload`: Use CPU offload for DiT inference. Enable if run out
  of memory.
- `--text-encoder-cpu-offload`: Use CPU offload for text encoder inference.
- `--vae-cpu-offload`: Use CPU offload for VAE.
- `--pin-cpu-memory`: Pin memory for CPU offload. Only added as a temp
  workaround if it throws “CUDA error: invalid argument”.

4.2.3 Known LoRA examples      *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-4-2-3-known-lora-examples*

Use `--lora-path` at startup or the
|sglang-diffusion-api-openai_api-lora-management| to load an adapter. Known
FLUX examples include:

- `dvyio/flux-lora-simple-illustration`
  https://huggingface.co/dvyio/flux-lora-simple-illustration
- `XLabs-AI/flux-furry-lora` https://huggingface.co/XLabs-AI/flux-furry-lora
- `XLabs-AI/flux-RealismLora`
  https://huggingface.co/XLabs-AI/flux-RealismLora

                         *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-4-2-4-negative-prompts-true-cfg*
4.2.4 Negative prompts (true CFG)

FLUX.1-dev is guidance-distilled: its guidance embedding comes from the
server's `--embedded-cfg-scale` (3.5 for FLUX.1), and the requests above run
one DiT forward per step. A negative prompt turns on classifier-free
guidance, which adds an unconditional forward per step:

- `--true-cfg-scale` sets the CFG weight, as in diffusers `FluxPipeline`.
- Without `--true-cfg-scale`, the negative prompt uses `--guidance-scale` as
  the CFG weight.

>bash
    sglang generate \
      --model-path black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-dev \
      --prompt "A red bicycle leaning against a stone wall beside a quiet lake at sunset" \
      --negative-prompt "blurry, distorted, low quality" \
      --true-cfg-scale 2.0 \
      --width 1024 --height 1024 --num-inference-steps 50 --seed 42
<
Keep the weight moderate. On top of the distilled guidance, CFG over-exposes
FLUX.1-dev in diffusers as well as in SGLang: at 1024x1024 and 28 steps,
about 45% of pixels clip to 0 or 255 at a weight of 4, and 8-15% at 1.5-2.

6. RUN IN COMFYUI                       *diffusion-FLUX-FLUX-6-run-in-comfyui*

----------------------------------------------------------------------------
HY-Nomas                                  *sglang-diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas*

Serve the HY-Nomas event-conditioned video world model with
SGLang-diffusion, as offline video jobs or a realtime WebSocket session.

1. QUICK START                     *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-1-quick-start*

Follow the |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation| on Linux with NVIDIA
CUDA.

One `sglang serve` process answers both HTTP video jobs and realtime
WebSocket sessions. On first load, SGLang downloads the official checkpoints
and assembles a local pipeline; see |diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-weights|.

2. MODEL CAPABILITIES       *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-2-model-capabilities*

HY-Nomas https://huggingface.co/tencent/Hy-Nomas is Tencent Hunyuan's video
world model. A video is described as a sequence of events: each event is a
structured JSON caption with a frame count and optional camera poses. The
model generates one event at a time while attending to the cached state of
earlier events, so a scene can be extended or redirected without
regenerating what came before.

Choose it for long, steerable scene continuation, either as one offline
request with all events planned up front or as a live session that receives
events one by one. It generates from noise: there is no image-to-video
input. Captions must follow the official event JSON schema, which the
official prompt enhancer (Nomas-PE) produces from free text; SGLang does not
host that enhancer.

The code and weights are released under Apache-2.0. See the
GitHub repository https://github.com/Tencent-Hunyuan/Hy-Nomas for the event
schema and training details.

Component     Implementation
------------  ----------------------------------------------------------------
Text encoder  Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct, 1280 tokens per event
DiT           Distilled segment-autoregressive DiT, 8 Euler steps, no CFG
Decoder       PixelUMM pixel decoder, windowed over 10 latents with 5-latent
              overlap
Camera        Optional normalized extrinsics, one matrix per latent-aligned
              frame

3. DEPLOYMENT                       *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-3-deployment*

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    sglang serve --model-path tencent/Hy-Nomas --num-gpus 1 --port 30000
<
For two GPUs, use Ulysses sequence parallelism. Its output matched the
single-GPU output pixel for pixel:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    sglang serve --model-path tencent/Hy-Nomas --num-gpus 2 --sp-degree 2 --port 30000
<
Tensor parallelism (`--tp-size 2`) also runs, but its trained-weight output
differs from one GPU, so prefer SP. `tp_size * sp_degree` must divide the 16
attention heads, and TP and SP can be combined. Ring attention, CFG
parallelism and data parallelism are rejected. Both layouts shard only the
DiT computation; the text encoder and decoder are not placed on a separate
GPU.

To reduce DiT memory, stream its blocks from host memory:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    sglang serve --model-path tencent/Hy-Nomas --num-gpus 1 --dit-layerwise-offload true --port 30000
<

Weights                                  *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-weights*

`tencent/Hy-Nomas` is resolved through a pinned model overlay
https://huggingface.co/Niehen6174/Hy-Nomas-overlay. It downloads the
official distilled, non-quantized DiT, the PixelUMM decoder and the
Qwen2.5-VL text encoder. It then caches a Diffusers-style pipeline under
`SGLANG_DIFFUSION_CACHE_ROOT`. Weights keep their original values and
dtypes. Later starts reuse the cache, and a local directory with the same
layout can be passed to `--model-path` instead. The first load needs extra
disk space for the decoder export.

The overlay does not include Nomas-PE, LoRA adapters, or FP8/INT8 variants.
To use the official quantized checkpoints, override the component weights as
shown below.

INT8 DiT and FP8 VAE        *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-int8-dit-and-fp8-vae*

On Linux with NVIDIA CUDA, you can use the official INT8 DiT and FP8 VAE
checkpoints from tencent/Hy-Nomas
https://huggingface.co/tencent/Hy-Nomas/tree/main. The FP8 VAE requires an
SM89 or newer GPU, such as an RTX 4090, and BF16 compute precision.

Download the two files with the Hugging Face CLI:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    hf download tencent/Hy-Nomas \
      DiT/distilled_int8/diffusion_pytorch_model_ema_0_995_int8.safetensors \
      VAE/PixelUMM_FT_FP8/diffusion_pytorch_model_fp8.safetensors \
      --local-dir ./hy-nomas-quantized
<
Start a single-GPU server with both component weight overrides:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path tencent/Hy-Nomas \
      --num-gpus 1 \
      --transformer-weights-path ./hy-nomas-quantized/DiT/distilled_int8/diffusion_pytorch_model_ema_0_995_int8.safetensors \
      --vae-weights-path ./hy-nomas-quantized/VAE/PixelUMM_FT_FP8/diffusion_pytorch_model_fp8.safetensors \
      --component-precisions.vae bf16 \
      --dit-layerwise-offload true \
      --port 30000
<
SGLang detects the quantization format from checkpoint metadata; no
additional quantization flag is needed. INT8 DiT weights are dequantized to
BF16 for linear computation, while the VAE uses FP8 GEMM. You can use either
weight override independently. The requests in the following sections work
with these checkpoints.

                   *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-4-offline-and-http-generation*
4. OFFLINE AND HTTP GENERATION

A request lists its events in `segments`. Each caption is a JSON string in
the official event schema; later events usually describe only what changes.
This two-event request makes 240 frames at 352×640 and saves it as
`request.json`:

[Python]

[Python]Python

>python
    import json

    first = {
        "Style": "Cinematic fantasy",
        "perspective_view": "Third-person view",
        "subject_list": [{"subject_id": "fox", "display_name": "a small orange fox",
                          "subject_attribute": "Orange fur, white chest, and a fluffy tail."}],
        "segments": [{
            "visual_description_long": "A small orange fox stands on a stone path in a luminous forest.",
            "contained_actions": [{"start_time": "00:00.000", "end_time": "00:05.000",
                                   "action_description": "The fox walks slowly along the path."}],
        }],
    }
    second = {
        "segments": [{
            "description_change": "Glowing blue flowers open beside the path.",
            "contained_actions": [{"start_time": "00:00.000", "end_time": "00:05.000",
                                   "action_description": "The fox passes the flowers and looks around."}],
        }],
    }
    request = {
        "prompt": json.dumps(first),
        "prompt_format": "json",
        "segments": [
            {"frames": 120, "caption": json.dumps(first)},
            {"frames": 120, "caption": json.dumps(second)},
        ],
        "num_frames": 240,
        "height": 352,
        "width": 640,
        "fps": 24,
        "num_inference_steps": 8,
        "guidance_scale": 1.0,
        "seed": 42,
    }
    with open("request.json", "w") as f:
        json.dump(request, f)
<
Send it to the HTTP video API, then poll `GET /v1/videos/{id}` and download
from `GET /v1/videos/{id}/content`:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    curl -sS http://127.0.0.1:30000/v1/videos \
      -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
      --data-binary @request.json
<
The same request runs without a server through the Python API:

[Python]

[Python]Python

>python
    from sglang.multimodal_gen import DiffGenerator

    with DiffGenerator.from_pretrained(model_path="tencent/Hy-Nomas") as generator:
        result = generator.generate({**request, "save_output": True})
<

Field                               Meaning
----------------------------------  ------------------------------------------
segments                            One entry per event: frames and a JSON
                                    caption, optionally norm_extrinsics. Event
                                    frames must sum to num_frames, and prompt
                                    repeats the first caption.
prompt_format                       json applies the official event schema.
                                    plain accepts free text for smoke tests
                                    only and logs a warning.
norm_extrinsics                     Camera matrices that are already
                                    normalized. The server does not normalize
                                    raw poses.
num_inference_steps,                The distilled checkpoint uses 8 steps and
guidance_scale                      guidance 1.
height, width                       Rounded to multiples of 32 as in the
                                    official code; 720×1280 becomes 704×1280.

Event frame counts do not need to be multiples of four; the decoded video is
cropped to each requested count. Sampling noise is always drawn on the CPU,
so a fixed seed gives the same video through the Python API and HTTP.

5. REALTIME SESSION           *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-5-realtime-session*

Open `ws://HOST:PORT/v1/realtime_video/generate` and exchange MessagePack
maps.

Message                           Direction  Content
--------------------------------  ---------  ---------------------------------
{"type": "init", "prompt": ...,   client     Starts the session. Without
"num_frames": ..., "size":                   segments, prompt and num_frames
"640x352",                                   form the first event.
"num_inference_steps": 8,
"condition_inputs":
{"prompt_format": "json",
"segments": [...]}}
{"type": "event", "kind":         client     Queues the next event.
"segment", "event_id": n,                    norm_extrinsics covers this event
"payload": {"caption": ...,                  only.
"frames": ...,
"norm_extrinsics": ...}}
{"type": "event", "kind": "end"}  client     No more events. The server sends
                                             the remaining frames and closes.
frame_batch / frame_batch_header  server     Raw RGB, WebP or JPEG frames for
                                             one chunk. A chunk may contain no
                                             frames.

Each event is generated as soon as it arrives, against the session's cached
history. The decoder streams in overlapping windows and sends a frame only
when no later window can change it, so frames are never revised. Output
therefore lags generation, and `end` releases the tail: a 120 + 120 frame
session sends 84, 120 and then 36 frames.

The server keeps one session at a time and rejects a second connection.
Disconnecting ends the session and frees its state; there is no resume.
Because a session draws noise from a persistent bank, compare its frames
with another session using the same seed, not with an offline request.

A minimal client sends the first event in `init`, the rest as `segment`
events, then `end`, and collects raw RGB frames until the server closes the
socket:

[Python]

[Python]Python

>python
    import msgspec
    import numpy as np
    import websocket

    events = request["segments"]  # from the request above
    ws = websocket.create_connection("ws://127.0.0.1:30000/v1/realtime_video/generate")
    ws.send_binary(msgspec.msgpack.encode({
        "type": "init",
        "prompt": events[0]["caption"],
        "num_frames": events[0]["frames"],
        "size": "640x352",
        "fps": 24,
        "num_inference_steps": 8,
        "seed": 42,
        "realtime_output_format": "raw",
        "condition_inputs": {"prompt_format": "json", "segments": events[:1]},
    }))
    for event_id, event in enumerate(events[1:], start=1):
        ws.send_binary(msgspec.msgpack.encode(
            {"type": "event", "kind": "segment", "event_id": event_id, "payload": event}))
    ws.send_binary(msgspec.msgpack.encode({"type": "event", "kind": "end"}))

    frames = []
    while True:
        try:
            raw = ws.recv()
        except websocket.WebSocketConnectionClosedException:
            break
        if not isinstance(raw, bytes):
            break
        message = msgspec.msgpack.decode(raw)
        if message.get("type") == "error":
            raise RuntimeError(message)
        if message.get("type") in ("frame_batch", "frame_batch_header"):
            # Large batches send their payload as the next binary message.
            payload = message["payload"] if message["type"] == "frame_batch" else ws.recv()
            if message["num_frames"]:
                shape = (message["num_frames"], message["height"], message["width"], message["channels"])
                frames.append(np.frombuffer(payload, dtype=np.uint8).reshape(shape))
    video = np.concatenate(frames)  # 240 frames
<
To steer from free text, run Nomas-PE as a separate service. It turns the
user's text and recent frames into the next event JSON, which the client
then sends as the `caption`.

KV cache budget                  *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-kv-cache-budget*

The session cache starts at 65,536 tokens and grows by 1.5× up to 32 GiB, or
to free GPU memory minus 8 GiB, whichever is smaller. At that limit, the
oldest event is evicted first, as in the official code. Setting
`max_cache_segments` also limits the number of cached events. Positions keep
counting across evictions. If a cache write fails after an eviction, the
session must be restarted.

These limits are `NomasPipelineConfig` fields
(`interactive_kv_cache_initial_tokens`,
`interactive_kv_cache_growth_factor`, `max_cache_gib`,
`cache_free_memory_reserve_gib`, `max_cache_segments`). At 1280×704, one
10-latent event uses about 4.5 GB of K/V on one GPU; TP and SP divide this
across ranks.

6. LIMITATIONS                     *diffusion-HY-Nomas-HY-Nomas-6-limitations*

- No image-to-video, classifier-free guidance, or raw camera-pose
  normalization.
- Nomas-PE is not served by SGLang.
- One realtime session per server; Ring attention, KV CPU swap, INT8 KV and
  sliding-window history are not implemented.
- TP output is not yet equivalent to single-GPU output with trained weights.
- Validation was on H200 only.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------
HiDream-O1-Image                   *sglang-diffusion-HiDream-HiDream-O1-Image*

Deploy HiDream-O1-Image with SGLang Diffusion.

1. QUICK START              *diffusion-HiDream-HiDream-O1-Image-1-quick-start*

Follow the |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation|, then choose a recipe
below. TP 1 on one H200 and TP 2 on two H200s in a single node are the
verified shapes.

                     *diffusion-HiDream-HiDream-O1-Image-2-model-capabilities*
2. MODEL CAPABILITIES

HiDream-O1-Image https://huggingface.co/HiDream-ai/HiDream-O1-Image is an 8B
pixel-level Unified Transformer (UiT). Unlike the usual latent-diffusion
layout, it has neither a VAE nor a separate text encoder: one Qwen3-VL
backbone embeds the prompt and predicts clean 32x32 RGB patches directly,
and the flow-matching head reads its timestep from a dedicated token in the
prompt. It loads through SGLang's native `HiDreamO1ImagePipeline`.

Choose it for text-to-image at its trained 2K-class resolutions, including
legible in-image text. The checkpoint ships a stock `qwen3_vl` config with
no `model_index.json`, so the pipeline loads the transformer itself; the
tradeoff is a narrower feature surface: bf16 only, one prompt per request,
no sequence parallelism, and monolithic serving only.

Model              Hugging Face model ID            Notes
-----------------  -------------------------------  --------------------------
HiDream-O1-Image   HiDream-ai/HiDream-O1-Image      Text-to-image, bf16 only

3. GENERATE AN IMAGE  *diffusion-HiDream-HiDream-O1-Image-3-generate-an-image*

One-shot generation from the CLI:

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    sglang generate \
      --model-path HiDream-ai/HiDream-O1-Image \
      --prompt "A cat is sitting next to a sign that says 'HiDream-O1'" \
      --num-inference-steps 50 \
      --guidance-scale 5.0 \
      --save-output
<
Or through the OpenAI-compatible image generation API once the server is up:

[Python]

[Python]Python

>python
    import base64
    from openai import OpenAI

    client = OpenAI(api_key="EMPTY", base_url="http://127.0.0.1:30010/v1")

    response = client.images.generate(
        model="HiDream-ai/HiDream-O1-Image",
        prompt="A cinematic photo of a quiet lakeside cabin at sunrise",
        n=1,
        response_format="b64_json",
    )

    image_bytes = base64.b64decode(response.data[0].b64_json)
    with open("hidream_o1.png", "wb") as f:
        f.write(image_bytes)
<

                     *diffusion-HiDream-HiDream-O1-Image-4-configuration-tips*
4. CONFIGURATION TIPS

- The model is released in bfloat16 only; `--dit-precision` /
  `--component-precisions` overrides are rejected rather than silently
  downcast.
- The trained resolutions are 2048x2048, 2304x1728, 2560x1440, 2496x1664,
  3104x1312, 2304x1792 and their transposes. Nothing snaps: an off-grid size
  is warned about in the server log and then rendered exactly as requested,
  and because pixel patches carry no scale invariance the quality degrades
  badly.
- Sequence parallelism is unsupported: the prompt span is causal while the
  image span is bidirectional, which needs a dense `[B, 1, S, S]` mask that
  cannot be sharded along the sequence. Use `--tp-size` for multi-GPU
  instead. `--cfg-parallel-size 2` is accepted at the default
  `--guidance-scale 5.0`, since it splits the two CFG branches across GPUs,
  but it has not been verified end to end.
- Encoder/denoiser/decoder disaggregation does not apply --- one component
  does all three, so the pipeline only runs in the monolithic role.
- The defaults are the reference `full` recipe: 50 steps,
  `--guidance-scale 5.0`, shift 3.0 (fixed). Classifier-free guidance costs
  a second forward pass per step, so `--guidance-scale 1.0` halves the step
  cost at some quality loss.
- Noise is drawn on the host, so a given `--seed` reproduces the same image
  on any accelerator.

5. RUN IN COMFYUI        *diffusion-HiDream-HiDream-O1-Image-5-run-in-comfyui*

----------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ideogram 4                               *sglang-diffusion-Ideogram-Ideogram4*

Deploy Ideogram 4 with SGLang Diffusion for high-aesthetic text-to-image
generation.

1. QUICK START                    *diffusion-Ideogram-Ideogram4-1-quick-start*

Follow the |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation| for your GPU
platform.

Set `HF_TOKEN` with access to the gated Ideogram repositories. Install
`bitsandbytes>=0.46.1` and `accelerate>=1.1.0` for NF4 and the shared NF4
text encoder.

                 *diffusion-Ideogram-Ideogram4-installation-and-prerequisites*
Installation and prerequisites

  - NVIDIA CUDA GPU.
  - SGLang installed with diffusion dependencies.
  - `bitsandbytes>=0.46.1` and `accelerate>=1.1.0` for the NF4 checkpoint
    and the fal variants' shared NF4 text encoder.
  - `HF_TOKEN` with access to the Ideogram 4 gated repositories.

2. MODEL CAPABILITIES      *diffusion-Ideogram-Ideogram4-2-model-capabilities*

Ideogram 4 https://huggingface.co/ideogram-ai/ideogram-4-nf4 is a
text-to-image family focused on polished composition and typography-aware
generation. SGLang supports the official NF4 and FP8 checkpoints,
Comfy-Org's NVFP4 transformer, and fal's distilled Fast and Instant
variants.

Choose NF4 when memory is the primary constraint, FP8/NVFP4 for supported
accelerator deployments, Fast for a 20-step balance, and Instant for the
8-step latency target. The public repositories are gated, and the available
releases are already quantized or distilled rather than a single
full-precision baseline.

Variant  Hugging Face model ID       Notes
-------  --------------------------  -----------------------------------------
NF4      ideogram-ai/ideogram-4-nf4  Official bitsandbytes NF4 checkpoint. Use
                                     this path first for low-memory
                                     deployment.
FP8      ideogram-ai/ideogram-4-fp8  Official FP8 checkpoint.
NVFP4    Comfy-Org/Ideogram-4        Comfy-Org NVFP4 transformer weights.
                                     SGLang loads non-transformer components
                                     from ideogram-ai/ideogram-4-fp8.
Fast     fal/ideogram-v4-fast        20-step, CFG-distilled, FP4-targeted
                                     floating checkpoint. Defaults to
                                     V4_FAST_20.
Instant  fal/ideogram-v4-instant     8-step, CFG- and timestep-distilled BF16
                                     checkpoint. Defaults to V4_INSTANT_8.

The fal repositories contain only the transformer component. When either
model ID is passed directly to `--model-path`, SGLang loads the text
encoder, tokenizer, VAE, and scheduler from the
`ideogram-ai/ideogram-4-nf4-diffusers` revision referenced by fal's model
cards, then runs the distilled transformer without the unconditional branch.
