6. RUN IN COMFYUI                       *diffusion-MOVA-MOVA-6-run-in-comfyui*

----------------------------------------------------------------------------
MiniMax-H3-Image                   *sglang-diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image*

Run native six-step MiniMax-H3-Image generation and image editing with
SGLang Diffusion.

1. QUICK START              *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-1-quick-start*

Use the SGLang main branch https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/tree/main
with the |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation|, then install its
diffusion dependencies from the repository root:

>bash
    python3 -m pip install -e 'python[diffusion]'
<
The public checkpoints are `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3-Image` and its
`MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3` text encoder. Run the commands from the same checkout
and Python environment. The picker saves the returned image as
`generated.png`. These recipes use Linux CUDA and a source installation; DGX
Spark uses the |diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-dgx-spark-128-gb|, and
AMD Instinct GPUs use the
|diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-amd-instinct-mi300x-mi325x-mi350x-mi355x|.

For the lowest measured latency, see the
|diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-measured-text-to-image-summary|: two
B300s generate a 4K image in 2.654 s at native precision or 2.065 s with
opt-in MXFP8. These are warm pipeline times; quantization can change image
quality.

Server warmup runs a valid prefix of the PDD schedule; real requests always
run all six steps. With online AdaLN and enough cache slots, warmup prepares
the full grids for both declared T2I and editing tasks without extra
denoising steps. Smaller custom caches prepare only the warmup task. New
shapes can still initialize kernels. When every plan fits on the GPU, Auto
omits the pinned host cache; an explicit `--minimax-h3-adaln-host-cache-gb`
overrides this. `--warmup-mode off` skips this preparation. For CLI/API
options, see the |sglang-sglang-diffusion-api-cli| and
|sglang-sglang-diffusion-api-openai_api|.

                     *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-2-model-capabilities*
2. MODEL CAPABILITIES

MiniMax-H3-Image https://huggingface.co/MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3-Image uses one
native checkpoint for text-to-image generation and image editing. An edit's
multipart `image` fields preserve reference order; repeat the field to
supply multiple references. The prompt refers to them as `<Picture 1>`,
`<Picture 2>`, and so on. The root checkpoint selects the image pipeline
automatically; `--model-variant` is not needed.

The DiT has about 33.1 billion parameters. Its separate Qwen3-VL encoder
checkpoint has about 33.4 billion; downloading both checkpoints and the VAE
requires about 135 GB. The pipeline loads only the encoder layers needed by
this release. Runtime memory also depends on placement, projection reuse and
the generation canvas.

Prompt text uses no chat template or system prompt and is truncated after
4096 tokens. Use a complete visual description for generation or editing.

The checkpoint has six fused PDD output heads and an explicit sigma grid.
Omit `num_inference_steps`, or set it to 6. It runs one positive branch:
omit guidance parameters and `negative_prompt`. It returns one image per
request. Audio and video inputs and outputs are not supported.

Task           Endpoint                Input
-------------  ----------------------  ---------------------------------------
Text to image  /v1/images/generations  JSON prompt
Image editing  /v1/images/edits        Multipart prompt and ordered image
                                       files

                *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-resolution-and-references*
Resolution and references

Recommended output sizes are:

Aspect ratio                                          Landscape    Portrait
----------------------------------------------------  -----------  -----------
1:1                                                   4096 × 4096  ---
4:3                                                   4736 × 3552  3552 × 4736
3:2                                                   5024 × 3360  3360 × 5024
16:9                                                  5472 × 3072  3072 × 5472
21:9                                                  6272 × 2688  ---

The default minimum generation area is 2048 × 2048 pixels. Smaller requests
are generated on a larger canvas with the same aspect ratio, aligned to 32
pixels, then resized to the requested output size with LANCZOS. A 1024 ×
1024 output therefore has the generation cost of a 2048 × 2048 canvas.

`min_generation_area` is a request-level integer pixel area between 1024 and
16777216. Reducing it changes the generation workload and potentially image
quality; it is not a lossless acceleration of the default recipe. Set
`min_generation_area` to `null` in a pipeline configuration file to disable
canvas enlargement; output dimensions must then be multiples of 32.

Edit references are oriented using EXIF and resized separately for each
encoder. Qwen uses a 1024-pixel short edge; the VAE keeps the short edge
within 1024--2048 pixels. Reference order, dimensions, generator device,
model revision and precision all matter when reproducing an edit.

                     *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-3-deployment-options*
3. DEPLOYMENT OPTIONS

DiT and text-encoder weights use BF16. The VAE checkpoint uses FP32; its
decoder weights are prepared in BF16 for BF16 autocast. The DiT uses dense
FlashAttention. Keep these precision and attention settings when reproducing
the release. ROCm has no FA3 build, so on AMD GPUs the DiT runs the same
dense packed attention through AITER. It is selected automatically and `fa`
is rejected there. Ring parallelism requires FA; use Ulysses or TP on AMD.

On discrete GPUs, Auto keeps all components resident when each GPU has at
least 130 GiB available at startup, as tested on H200, B300, MI325X and
MI350X. Otherwise it retains component offload. GB10 uses the shared-memory
policy in the Spark recipe below. Explicit placement overrides take
precedence. Resident mode requires space for all weights and request
activations; sequence parallelism still replicates weights. TP shards the
DiT projections. Ulysses and Ring split the packed sequence; choose degrees
compatible with the 56 attention heads. The picker marks unmeasured hardware
and option combinations as unverified. For two H200s or B300s, select
encoder fold. Ulysses2 has the lowest measured server pipeline latency; TP2
is also close and uses less memory.

On H200, B300, GB10 and RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell with standard weights, Auto
precomputes the native AdaLN timestep projections and releases their
weights. This retains the original precision and outputs, reducing measured
peak reserved memory by about 24.6--24.7 GiB on one GPU. Text-to-image and
editing use separate plans; keep the checkpoint files available for uncached
plans. The bounded GPU and host caches reuse these exact results. Select
Keep weights in the picker, or pass `--minimax-h3-adaln-online false`, to
avoid the rebuild cost. Other GPUs keep projection weights by default;
explicit online precomputation is available through Precompute.

`quality=lossless` is the default. It enables shared DiT and VAE
QK-norm/RoPE fusion, uses cuDNN SDPA in the VAE with a backend fallback, and
batches existing overlapping VAE decode tiles, with at most 128 samples per
tile batch. With multiple GPUs, complete tiles run across the decode group
before assembly; tile geometry and overlap stay unchanged. Select Local
tiles in the picker or pass `--vae-config.use-parallel-tiling false` to run
all tiles on each GPU. `quality=exact` restores the release's fusion and
sequential tile path. Neither changes the six-step schedule or canvas.
Floating-point reordering can change pixels; lossless does not mean
byte-identical output across paths or topologies. Text refinement and rotary
tables are prepared once per request for both paths. The shared conditioning
cache defaults to 512 MiB per worker. Matching prompts and ordered reference
content can reuse text conditioning; matching VAE inputs can reuse posterior
moments, including with multiple GPUs. Sampling still uses the request's
generator. Pass `--disable-conditioning-cache` to disable reuse across
requests. These recipes retain the release checkout's library numerics
defaults, including cuDNN TF32 and BF16 reduced-precision reduction. The
stricter overrides
`--allow-cudnn-tf32 false --allow-bf16-reduced-precision-reduction false`
are available but are not covered by these measurements.

Layerwise offload streams the DiT, text encoder and VAE decoder blocks from
host RAM while preserving native precision. Use Precompute with it to avoid
reloading the large AdaLN projection weights. This is the capacity option
for smaller GPUs; resident serving remains faster when the model fits.

Online FP8 / MXFP8 quantizes the DiT, optionally with the text encoder, from
the original checkpoint. Select it separately in the picker; native BF16
remains the default. These are approximate modes even with `quality=exact`
or `lossless`: quality controls kernel execution within the selected
precision. Quantization can change composition and reference identity, so
compare representative edits before choosing it. AdaLN precomputation and
the VAE retain native precision. MXFP8 uses block scaling on Blackwell and
the FP8 fallback on Hopper; use FP8 on older supported GPUs.

FP8 attention separately quantizes attention inputs on SM120/SM121 GPUs.
Select it explicitly in the picker, or pass
`--component-attention-backends.transformer fp8_fa_sm120`. Native
FlashAttention remains the default. The measured Spark combination uses
MXFP8 DiT + encoder and FP8 attention together; both change numerical
precision. Ring parallelism requires native FlashAttention.

Breakable CUDA graph captures the picker's task, size and reference layout
during startup. Its warmup prompt matches the generated request; editing
also needs the reference files on the server. Matching signatures replay the
graph, while other signatures run eager. Regional compile compiles repeated
DiT blocks with PyTorch. Both keep all six steps; compile can change
floating-point rounding and increases cold startup time. Graphs also work
with layerwise offload; capture retains device buffers and needs more GPU
memory than eager streaming. Eager remains the default.

                 *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-hardware-recommendations*
Hardware recommendations

The picker provides measured recipes for H200/B300, AMD Instinct,
single-card RTX PRO 6000 and DGX Spark, and Derived recipes for other listed
GPUs. A derived recipe estimates placement and parallel layout for the
selected GPU count from checkpoint sizes and measured H200 memory use. It
has no hardware latency measurement or capacity guarantee. Apply Use derived
recipe to set its placement, encoder fold and online AdaLN together. Start
with 2048 × 2048.

GPU class                 Default shape              Recommended placement and
                                                     layout
------------------------  -------------------------  -------------------------
B200, GB200, GB300        1 GPU                      Auto resident; Ulysses as
                                                     GPU count grows
MI355X / MI350X, MI325X   1 GPU; Ulysses as GPU      Measured Auto resident
/ MI300X                  count grows                recipes on 1, 2, 4 and 8
                                                     GPUs; Ulysses is faster,
                                                     TP uses less memory
H100 / H800, 80 GB        1 GPU, or 2 for resident   Component offload on one;
                          serving                    resident Ulysses2 with
                                                     encoder fold on two
RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell,   1 GPU                      Measured resident recipe
96 GB                                                with AdaLN
                                                     precomputation; component
                                                     offload saves memory
DGX Spark, 128 GB shared  1 GB10                     Measured Auto resident
                                                     recipe with native AdaLN
                                                     precomputation; requires
                                                     at least 106 GiB
                                                     available
RTX 6000 Ada, RTX 5090 /  1 GPU                      Layerwise offload with
4090 and listed 12--48 GB                             native AdaLN
cards without NVLink                                 precomputation
RTX 3090, 24 GB           1 GPU; optionally a        Layerwise offload with
                          two-card NVLink pair       native AdaLN
                                                     precomputation

Multi-GPU recommendations assume an NVLink-connected machine. Cards without
NVLink use single-card recipes by default. Offload recipes assume a 256
GB-class host and local NVMe. Layerwise offload preserves the six steps,
native precision and minimum generation canvas. The capacity validation uses
a constrained B300 allocator: native 2K/4K generation and editing, including
two-reference 4K, passed with an 8 GiB allocator limit and a 10.01 GiB
measured whole-GPU peak. It is not a latency measurement on the listed
consumer GPUs.

 *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-amd-instinct-mi300x-mi325x-mi350x-mi355x*
AMD Instinct (MI300X, MI325X, MI350X, MI355X)

Select an AMD GPU in the picker for single-node recipes on 1, 2, 4 and 8
GPUs. MI355X and MI350X (gfx950) share one recipe; MI325X and MI300X
(gfx942) share another. The recipes were run on MI350X and MI325X. No
attention flag is needed: the DiT selects AITER on ROCm. Auto placement
keeps every component resident, and AdaLN projections keep their weights.
Ring is not available; use Ulysses or TP. At each GPU count, Ulysses has the
lowest measured latency and TP the lowest memory per GPU.

Install this checkout over the SGLang in an SGLang ROCm image for your GPU:

GPU             Tested image                           Python, PyTorch
--------------  -------------------------------------  -----------------------
MI355X, MI350X  lmsysorg/sglang-rocm:v0.5.23-rocm720-  3.10, 2.9.1+rocm7.2.0
                mi35x-20261109
MI325X, MI300X  lmsysorg/sglang-rocm:v0.5.23-rocm10-   3.12, 2.11.0+rocm10.0.0
                mi30x-20261107

>bash
    docker run -it --network host --ipc host --shm-size 32g \
      --device /dev/kfd --device /dev/dri --group-add video \
      --security-opt seccomp=unconfined \
      -v "$PWD":/workspace/sglang -v "$HOME/.cache/huggingface":/root/.cache/huggingface \
      -e HF_TOKEN \
      lmsysorg/sglang-rocm:v0.5.23-rocm720-mi35x-20261109 bash

    # Inside the container, from the SGLang checkout:
    cd /workspace/sglang
    python3 -m pip install -e python --no-deps --no-build-isolation
    # On the Python 3.10 MI35x image, add --ignore-requires-python.
<
The image already provides the ROCm builds of PyTorch, AITER and the
diffusion dependencies, so `--no-deps` keeps them in place. Then run the
picker's serving and request commands in this environment.

DGX Spark (128 GB)       *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-dgx-spark-128-gb*

Select DGX Spark for a verified single-node, single-GB10 recipe at 2K and
the measured 4K sizes. Use an Arm64 CUDA environment with GB10 support
before installing this checkout. At `539fea720b86` and later, Auto enables
native AdaLN precomputation and keeps components resident when at least 106
GiB is available in the shared pool. The picker therefore needs no placement
or AdaLN override. Resident GB10 rejects Keep weights, which would retain
the large projection tensors. Start with a 2048 × 2048 output.

The 128 GB configuration
https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/products/workstations/dgx-spark/ has one
shared CPU/GPU memory pool
https://docs.nvidia.com/dgx/dgx-spark/known-issues.html#guidance-for-reporting-memory-resources-with-unified-memory-architecture.
Component offload does not provide another 128 GB of memory. Whole-component
transfers can temporarily hold both CPU and GPU copies in that pool. Use a
bounded container with swap disabled for the process, such as
`--memory 104g --memory-swap 104g --shm-size 8g`, and download weights
inside that container so its file cache can be reclaimed within the limit.
The picker marks option combinations outside its measured scope Unverified.
The container limit does not cap all CUDA allocations; keep at least 106 GiB
available in the shared pool before starting the verified Auto recipe.

The tested ARM64 base is
`lmsysorg/sglang@sha256:d4a56f6f9356539957974bb4008161b4c08d14a712805ff63eae526f36dbda71`,
with driver 580.173.02, PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130 and FlashInfer 0.7.3. Mount
this checkout and a task-owned cache directory, then install the source and
matching diffusion dependencies inside the container:

>bash
    H3_IMAGE_CACHE="$HOME/.cache/h3-image-spark"
    umask 077
    mkdir -p "$H3_IMAGE_CACHE"
    docker run --platform linux/arm64 --gpus all \
      --memory 104g --memory-swap 104g --shm-size 8g --cpus 12 \
      -v "$PWD":/workspace/sglang -v "$H3_IMAGE_CACHE":/root/.cache/huggingface \
      -e HF_TOKEN -it \
      lmsysorg/sglang@sha256:d4a56f6f9356539957974bb4008161b4c08d14a712805ff63eae526f36dbda71 bash

    # Inside the container, from the SGLang checkout:
    cd /workspace/sglang
    export CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/h3-image-spark-build
    python3 -m pip install setuptools-rust setuptools-scm wheel
    python3 -m pip install --no-build-isolation -e 'python[diffusion]'
    python3 -m pip uninstall -y flashinfer-jit-cache-sm80 flashinfer-jit-cache-sm89 \
      flashinfer-jit-cache-sm90a flashinfer-jit-cache-sm100a flashinfer-jit-cache-sm103a \
      flashinfer-jit-cache-sm110a flashinfer-jit-cache-sm120f flashinfer-jit-cache-sm121a
    python3 -m pip install --no-deps flash-attn-4==4.0.0b38
    python3 -m pip install --no-deps flashinfer-cubin==0.7.3 \
      --index-url https://flashinfer.ai/whl/
    python3 -m pip install --no-deps flashinfer-jit-cache==0.7.3+cu130 \
      --index-url https://flashinfer.ai/whl/cu130
<
This replaces the base image's incompatible optional kernel providers while
retaining FlashInfer's version checks. Download weights and run the picker's
serving/request commands in this environment. The measured container uses 12
CPU cores; the host's 128 GB is a shared pool, not 128 GB of additional
VRAM.

4. MEASURED RECIPES    *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-4-measured-recipes*

           *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-measured-text-to-image-summary*
Measured text-to-image summary

The native rows keep the checkpoint's release precision. FP8 and MXFP8
quantize the DiT and text encoder; they can change image details and
reference identity. Here, 2K is 2048 × 2048 and 4K is 4736 × 3552.

Hardware                                          Precision  2K       4K
------------------------------------------------  ---------  -------  --------
2 × B300                                          Native     0.604 s  2.654 s
2 × B300                                          MXFP8      0.451 s  2.065 s
2 × H200                                          Native     1.123 s  5.465 s
1 × H200                                          Native     1.934 s  10.133 s
1 × RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell                        Native     3.434 s  18.544 s
1 × RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell                        FP8        2.338 s  14.445 s
1 × DGX Spark                                     Native     16.21 s  84.96 s
8 × MI350X                                        Native     0.490 s  2.110 s
4 × MI325X                                        Native     0.890 s  3.680 s
1 × MI325X                                        Native     2.190 s  10.750 s

In the picker, use Ulysses2 and encoder Fold for two GPUs. Choose Auto
placement for native two-GPU, Spark and AMD recipes, or Resident for the
other rows. The MI350X row uses TP8 and the four-MI325X row Ulysses4; MI355X
and MI300X use the MI350X and MI325X recipes. Select FP8 DiT + encoder or
MXFP8 DiT + encoder only for the quantized rows.

All rows run the full six-step schedule with lossless quality, eager
execution, native AdaLN precomputation and warm conditioning caches. Times
are medians of warm server pipeline requests, excluding startup, HTTP
transport, PNG encoding and client saving; they are not first-request or
end-to-end latency. See the sections below for source revisions,
checkpoints, sample counts and environments.

                   *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-dgx-spark-at-2k-and-4k*
DGX Spark at 2K and 4K

One GB10 with Auto resident placement and native AdaLN precomputation, at
`539fea720b86`, using the pinned checkpoints and the same ARM64 base with
FlashInfer 0.7.2:

Workload                      Pipeline median       Observed warm range
----------------------------  --------------------  --------------------------
T2I, 2048 × 2048              16.21 s               15.65--16.80 s
Editing, 2048 × 2048          25.05 s               24.58--25.25 s
T2I, 4736 × 3552              84.96 s               81.51--87.97 s
Editing, 4096 × 4096          98.83 s               93.73--105.62 s
T2I, 6272 × 2688              81.04 s               79.36--85.95 s

Each cell uses 10 warm lossless requests across two fresh servers, excluding
the first lossless request per task in each. All six steps run at native
precision; conditioning is warm. T2I uses seed 42; 2K editing uses seed 42
and 4K editing uses seed 1234, with one reference. These are pipeline times,
excluding HTTP, PNG encoding and client saving. Maximum CUDA reserved memory
over the validated workflows was about 91 GiB; other runtime and host
allocations also share the same pool. The native offline API also passed 2K
generation and editing at exact and lossless quality with matching decoded
pixels.

Startup takes several minutes in the 12-core container, even with cached
checkpoints. Keep the server running to reuse weight loading and warmup;
both are excluded from the warm-request table.

   *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-dgx-spark-with-mxfp8-and-fp8-attention*
DGX Spark with MXFP8 and FP8 attention

Select MXFP8 DiT + encoder and FP8 attention for the accelerated Spark
recipe. Native precision remains the default; these options can change image
composition and details even with `quality=lossless`.

Workload                      Pipeline median       Observed warm range
----------------------------  --------------------  --------------------------
T2I, 2048 × 2048              10.99 s               10.86--11.14 s
T2I, 4736 × 3552              56.31 s               54.85--56.55 s
Editing, 4096 × 4096          62.78 s               62.75--62.82 s

Measured at `7e9078906472` and `78567c4042a0` with FlashInfer 0.7.3 and the
ARM64 environment above. 4K generation has five measured requests across two
fresh servers (three plus two); the other rows have three each. The first
request per task in each server is excluded as warmup. All six steps run
with resident components, eager execution and warm conditioning. T2I uses
seed 42; editing uses seed 1234 and one 1024 × 1024 reference. Times exclude
HTTP, PNG encoding and client saving. Peak CUDA reserved memory is 51.32 GiB
in the shared pool. The native baseline at `1b51a11129bb` on the same
environment measured 82.45 s for 4736 × 3552 (81.73--83.18 s, two warm
requests).

B300 at 2K                     *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-b300-at-2k*

Unquantized checkpoints at native precision, 2048 × 2048 generation canvas,
six steps, seed 42 and a CPU generator; one node of B300 GPUs. The image
revision is `7b3d20b4659f5d4b3ad7205daa008e47d6b1e868`, with H3 text encoder
revision `42ed227ee7df40d41602854ae760620d6eb651fe`. Source installation was
tested at `7ccff196eb3a` for the single-GPU row and `539fea720b86` for all
two-GPU rows, with PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130, FlashInfer 0.7.2 and Transformers
5.19.0. The two-GPU comparison uses an NV18-connected pair, parallel VAE
tiles and warm exact conditioning reuse.

GPUs and layout                            T2I pipeline    Editing pipeline
-----------------------------------------  --------------  -------------------
1 × B300, Auto resident                    0.994 s         1.570 s
2 × B300, TP2, encoder fold                0.621 s         0.978 s
2 × B300, Ulysses2, encoder fold           0.604 s         0.931 s
2 × B300, Ring2, encoder fold              0.692 s         1.093 s

All rows use lossless quality, resident placement and native AdaLN
precomputation. The single-GPU row is the median of six warm requests per
task across two server lifecycles, after two excluded requests per task in
each. Each two-GPU row uses 10 warm requests across two fresh servers,
excluding the first lossless request per task in each. Layout order is
balanced across the six server starts. Cold plan construction is excluded.
All use the checkpoint's example prompts and one edit reference. These are
server-reported pipeline latencies, excluding HTTP transport, PNG encoding
and client saving. They are not throughput measurements or results for other
GPUs. H100 and B200 recipes remain unmeasured.

B300 with MXFP8           *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-b300-with-mxfp8*

Select MXFP8 DiT + encoder, Resident, two B300s and Ulysses2 with encoder
fold. Online quantization changes numerics; native precision remains the
default. At `ff5dede83b0f`, a balanced native → MXFP8 → MXFP8 → native
comparison measured:

Workload                    Native pipeline      MXFP8 pipeline      Reduction
--------------------------  -------------------  ------------------  ---------
T2I, 2048 × 2048            0.604 s              0.451 s             25.4%
T2I, 4736 × 3552            2.653 s              2.065 s             22.2%
Editing, 4096 × 4096        3.146 s              2.490 s             20.9%

Each cell has 10 warm requests across two fresh servers, excluding the first
lossless request per task in each. Both paths use all six steps, warm exact
conditioning reuse, native AdaLN precomputation and parallel VAE tiles on
the same NV18 pair, checkpoints and dependencies. T2I uses seed 42; editing
uses seed 1234 and one reference. Times exclude HTTP, PNG encoding and
client saving. Maximum server-reported CUDA reserved memory across these
workloads fell from 69.2 GiB to 40.0 GiB; this is not total host or combined
multi-GPU memory.

MXFP8 DiT + encoder also passed 2K/4K generation, editing and ordered
multi-image requests on one DGX Spark. Choose that weight option in the
Spark picker; AdaLN and VAE keep native precision.

B300 at 4K                     *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-b300-at-4k*

Two B300s at `539fea720b86`, with encoder fold, Auto resident placement and
native AdaLN precomputation, using the same pinned checkpoints and
dependencies:

Layout    T2I, 4736 × 3552     Editing, 4096 × 4096        T2I, 6272 × 2688
--------  -------------------  --------------------------  -------------------
TP2       2.790 s              3.314 s                     2.795 s
Ulysses2  2.654 s              3.146 s                     2.662 s
Ring2     3.032 s              3.568 s                     3.026 s

Each cell uses 10 warm lossless requests across two fresh servers, excluding
the first lossless request per task in each. The same balanced layout
comparison covers 2K and 4K. All six steps run at native precision;
conditioning is warm. T2I uses seed 42; editing uses seed 1234 and one
reference. These are pipeline times, excluding HTTP, PNG encoding and client
saving. Ulysses2 is the fastest measured layout for these workloads; TP2
lowers maximum reserved memory across ranks and workflow phases to about
49.6 GiB, versus 69.2 GiB for Ulysses2 and 69.6 GiB for Ring2. These
allocator measurements exclude additional CUDA process memory.

             *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-rtx-pro-6000-blackwell-96-gb*
RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell, 96 GB

On one RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition, resident placement is faster
than component offload for the tested workloads. Both use native precision,
FA4, all six steps and AdaLN precomputation; select Resident in the picker.

Workload                 Resident pipeline    Component-offload pipeline
-----------------------  -------------------  --------------------------------
T2I, 2048 × 2048         3.434 s              5.094 s

Source `a9d721f36e16`, PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130, FlashInfer 0.7.2 and FA4
4.0.0b35; the same pinned checkpoints and example inputs as above. Each cell
uses 10 warm requests across two fresh servers in a resident/offload ABBA
comparison, excluding the first lossless request per task in each.
Conditioning is warm; HTTP transport, PNG encoding and client saving are
excluded. Maximum reserved memory over the 4K workflows was about 92.2 GiB
resident or 66.1 GiB with offload, before additional CUDA process memory.
Resident has limited headroom for other workloads; Auto retains conservative
component offload on this 96 GB device. The picker provides the single-card
recipe for this GPU, which has no NVLink.

For a smaller working set, select FP8 DiT + encoder with Resident. At
`5c7ce652b563`, a separate native → FP8 → FP8 → native comparison on the

Each cell has 10 warm requests across two fresh servers, excluding the first
lossless request per task in each. All six steps, warm conditioning, example
inputs, seeds and the pipeline timing boundary match the B300 quantization

H200 at 2K                     *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-h200-at-2k*

H200 measurements use the same checkpoints, prompts, precision, six-step
schedule and canvas. Single-GPU T2I uses `851f8cb71469` with explicit
resident placement and online AdaLN; these become Auto defaults at
`9979ece836fb`. Single-GPU editing uses `be38e34be99d`; Ulysses2 uses
`862194eeaedb` with parallel VAE tiles. Both use Auto placement and exact
conditioning reuse. TP2 and Ring2 use `9979ece836fb`. All two-GPU rows use
encoder fold.

-----------------------------------------  --------------  -------------------
1 × H200, resident                         1.934 s         3.116 s

2 × H200, Ring2, encoder fold              1.224 s         2.146 s

Single-GPU T2I uses 20 warm requests across four fresh servers, excluding

Ulysses2. These are latency measurements, not throughput results.

For the single-H200 editing workload, the picker defaults to exact
conditioning reuse. Disabling it with `--disable-conditioning-cache`
increased the warm pipeline median from 3.116 s to 3.446 s. Both settings
use eight measured requests across two fresh servers in an ABBA comparison,
with the first two requests excluded per server. All 24 output PNGs matched
byte for byte. Changing the prompt or references can miss the cache; these
are repeated-input measurements, not uncached editing latency.

PNG encoding at 4K     *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-png-encoding-at-4k*

Select No compression under Request options, or send `output_compression=0`,
to trade response size for less PNG encoding and decoding work. The default
compression stays unchanged. On two-H200 and two-B300 Ulysses2 recipes at
`862194eeaedb`, all decoded pixels matched for both formats:

                           saved-request    PNG,              default →
                           time             saved-request     uncompressed
                                            time

2 × H200  T2I, 4736 ×      8.140 s          7.211 s           31.7 → 50.5 MB
          3552
2 × H200  Editing, 4096 ×  8.838 s          8.258 s           22.7 → 50.4 MB
          4096

          2688

          4096

          2688

Each format uses eight measured requests per workload across two fresh
servers, with balanced request order and one excluded warmup per format in
each server. Timing runs from the loopback HTTP request through PNG saving
and pixel validation. Pipeline time stayed approximately unchanged; these
are codec/client-path savings. Larger payloads may take longer over a slower
network. Both formats retain native precision, all six steps and the same
generation canvas.

File URL delivery       *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-file-url-delivery*

Base64 remains the default and uses temporary output files. Select File URL
under Setup to add `--output-path` to the server command and save each
response through a separate image download. Restart the server after
switching. Files remain in the selected output directory until you remove
them; the URL records last only for that server process.

On two NVLink-connected B300s with Ulysses2 at `539fea720b86`, File URL
shortened the measured save path further when using No compression:

Workload               Base64, uncompressed PNG   File URL, uncompressed PNG
---------------------  -------------------------  ----------------------------
T2I, 4736 × 3552       4.380 s                    3.990 s
Editing, 4096 × 4096   4.982 s                    4.620 s
T2I, 6272 × 2688       4.397 s                    4.028 s

Both columns use the same persistent output directory, native precision, six
steps and warm conditioning. Each cell uses eight measured requests across
two fresh servers, excluding the first request per format/server, with
balanced format and compression order. Timing includes the POST, image
download, PNG saving and pixel validation. Default and uncompressed PNG both
retained identical decoded pixels across response formats. Pipeline latency
stayed unchanged. The extra HTTP request and larger PNGs can cost more on a
slower connection. Other hardware and workloads remain unverified for File
URL delivery.

B300 startup warmup   *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image-b300-startup-warmup*

Peak reserved CUDA memory was about 91 GiB on one B300. These results
include native fusion, VAE batching and exact projection reuse, without
reducing steps or generation area.

One-step server warmup at `f3c0a6f09c6b` prepares both task grids when the
online cache holds at least 12 plans and omits the unnecessary host copy.
Compared with `ea0cba698a08`, on the same cached checkpoints and one
resident B300:

First-request measurement                          Before     Current default
-------------------------------------------------  ---------  ----------------
First editing request, pipeline                    4.041 s    2.065 s
Process start to first editing HTTP response       38.3 s     35.4 s
Process start to first T2I HTTP response           36.4 s     34.0 s
Worker CPU RSS after first editing request         14.52 GiB  6.52 GiB

Each first-request scenario uses four fresh servers per path. Both-task
precomputation moves work into startup; avoiding the unused host tier saves
about 8 GiB of worker RSS and removes its allocation cost. The warm-request
table above uses a different timing boundary.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------
MiniMax-H3                               *sglang-diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3*

Run native MiniMax-H3 video-and-audio generation with SGLang Diffusion.

1. QUICK START                    *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-1-quick-start*

Follow the |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation|, then choose a
verified recipe below. Docker is recommended where the picker offers it.
Setup changes the deployment; Server and Request expose orthogonal startup
and sampling choices.

  Note: ~

  The generated Server command already includes the recommended encoder
  policy. Change a Server option only for a deliberate trade-off; Request
  options do not reload the model.

The Docker form uses the image's bundled diffusion dependencies without
reinstalling them at startup. For conditioned requests, set Host media
directory under Variables; the builder mounts it read-only at
`/data/minimax-h3`. AMD and Grace Blackwell currently offer the Python form;
the other NVIDIA recipes also offer Docker.

To use ModelScope through the same normal `sglang serve` path, prefix the
copied command with `SGLANG_USE_MODELSCOPE=true` and replace the model path
with `MiniMax/MiniMax-H3`. Keep the selected variant and topology flags
unchanged.

For platform-specific installation details, see the
|sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-installation|.

2. MODEL CAPABILITIES      *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-2-model-capabilities*

MiniMax-H3 https://huggingface.co/MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 is a native joint
video-and-audio model for text-to-video-and-audio, first/last-frame control,
and multimodal reference conditioning. Its main strength is producing the
picture and stereo soundtrack together, so speech, music, ambient sound, and
visible events can stay aligned without a separate audio-generation pass.

Choose H3 when synchronized audiovisual output or reference-driven
generation matters more than a lightweight deployment. The released recipe
targets a 768-pixel short edge at 24 fps for 4--15 seconds, and its
capabilities are split across two checkpoint partitions; serving every mode
therefore requires separate FL2VA and Ref2VA deployments, or
|diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-one-server-for-both-partitions|.

Task                              task value  Conditioning
--------------------------------  ----------  --------------------------------
Text to video and audio           t2va        Text prompt only
First/last frame to video and     fl2va       First frame, last frame, or both
audio
Reference to video and audio      ref2va      Image, video, and audio
                                              references, optionally combined
                                              with first/last keyframes

Video-to-video (V2V) is a supported `ref2va` use case, not a fourth task
value. Run the `Ref2VA` partition and provide a video reference in
`conditions`. A hybrid `ref2va` request may also include the same ordered
first/last keyframes accepted by `fl2va`, but it must still contain at least
one reference condition.

Use the selected Hub's root model ID: `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3` on Hugging Face
or `MiniMax/MiniMax-H3` on ModelScope. Select the checkpoint variant with
`--model-variant`: `fl2va` serves both `t2va` and `fl2va`, while `ref2va`
serves reference-conditioned requests, and `fl2va+ref2va` loads both
transformers into one server. SGLang owns the checkpoint-directory mapping;
do not point `--model-path` at a manually downloaded subdirectory. The same
FL2VA / Ref2VA split applies in
|diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-comfyui-integrated-mode|.

  Warning: ~

  Review the license and usage terms in the MiniMax-H3 model card before
  production or commercial use. SGLang support does not grant additional
  model usage rights.

Image checkpoints             *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-image-checkpoints*

For the separate six-step text-to-image and editing checkpoint, see
|sglang-diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-Image|.

3. DEPLOYMENT DETAILS      *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-3-deployment-details*

The builder accepts legal custom GPU counts and topologies, marking them
Unverified until the exact recipe has completed end-to-end validation.
Static H3 head or partition violations disable Copy before they reach
`sglang serve`.

Grace Blackwell                 *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-grace-blackwell*

Select GB300 or GB200 in the builder. The default is one Linux ARM64 host
with four GPUs, Ulysses4, and resident weights. This follows the
four-GPU compute-tray layout
https://docs.nvidia.com/dgx/dgxgb200-user-guide/hardware.html; an NVL72 rack
is not a single 72-GPU process host.

For two four-GPU hosts, set Nodes = 2 and GPUs / node = 4. The builder emits
Ulysses4 inside each host, Ring2 across hosts, and replicated encoders. Set
the same reachable Head address on both hosts and use node ranks 0 and

1. Run the generated command once per host, with the same SGLang revision
   and dependencies. Let `sglang serve` resolve and download the selected
   weights.

GB200 is a derived, unverified recipe, not a measured GB300-equivalent
performance claim. Keep the native precision and platform-default attention
for the baseline; the B200/B300 quantization measurements and H200 Cache-DiT
quality audit do not establish coverage on Grace Blackwell.

GB300 end-to-end coverage currently includes single-host FL2VA text-to-video
with audio, native precision, eager execution, and `quality="exact"`. Ref2VA
and multi-host commands remain Unverified. See the
|diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-gb300-single-host| for the tested workload.

Checkpoint and adapter formats

on the same native SGLang pipeline; component repositories contribute their
own config and weights, while weight files retain the base component config.
Storage layout and inference behavior are separate contracts: for example, a
PEFT file may be either a normal style adapter or a timestep-distilled Turbo
adapter.

The rows compose rather than enumerate every cross-product. A Turbo-merged
INT8 ConvRot checkpoint, for example, must satisfy both the Turbo sampling
contract and the ConvRot storage/backend contract.

H3's Qwen3-VL text encoder also handles image understanding; there is no
separate `image_encoder` component. Select the conditioning checkpoint, not
an optional generation-tail file for prompt rewriting. The `uncensored` or
`Heretic` label describes a weight modification, not a separate loader or a
guarantee of output quality.

For H3, the registered component names are `transformer`, `text_encoder`,
`video_vae`, and `audio_vae`. The shorter `--transformer-weights-path` and
`--text-encoder-path` aliases remain supported. `conditioning_projection` is
an H3 text-encoder sidecar key, not a standalone model component. Plain
video/audio VAE safetensors can use `--component-weights-paths.video_vae` or
`--component-weights-paths.audio_vae`, but SGLang does not currently
advertise a native quantized H3 VAE format.

Pre-quantized files are self-describing: do not combine those rows with
`--quantization` or `--component-quantizations.*`. Only byte-identical
official full weights---whether loaded from the original model, Diffusers
component, or a weight-only layout---belong to the consistency GT. Pruned,
quantized, compact-encoder, and LoRA routes are outside that baseline and
the audited `quality="high"` contract. Packed and per-layer mixed formats
reject FSDP unless their row says otherwise; see
|sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-quantization| for backend-wide constraints.

LoRA tensors alone do not make an execution-coupled release portable.
Sparse- attention/SLA adapters and causal-streaming adapters such as RAVEN
also require their matching attention or streaming pipeline; they are not
standard H3 LoRA overlays in SGLang. Likewise, a remixed checkpoint that
prescribes a custom sampler is only covered when that sampler contract can
be reproduced---the fact that its safetensors layout loads is not
sufficient.

For a four-card H200 host, keep the full BF16/FP32 model resident by
default. The model fits without FSDP, so this path avoids the per-block
parameter all-gathers of the memory-oriented FSDP profile:

[4×H200 resident]

[4×H200 resident]4×H200 resident

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 \
      --model-variant fl2va \
      --num-gpus 4 \
      --ulysses-degree 4 \
      --encoder-parallel auto \
      --performance-mode speed \
      --port 30010
<
Pure Ulysses4 is also the faster measured topology on H200, not just a
capacity default. The 4×H100 TP2 + Ulysses2 recipe below fits on 141 GB H200
cards, but it replaces the Ulysses all-to-all exchange with two per-block
tensor-parallel all-reduces and measured slower end-to-end, at about 30 GB
lower peak memory per GPU. See the H200 topology comparison in the
Benchmarks section for the measured numbers; treat TP2 + Ulysses2 on H200 as
a deliberate memory trade, not a latency default.

For 4×H100 80 GB, balance the large packed activation with resident weight
sharding. TP2 + Ulysses2 was the fastest measured lossless topology while
the Qwen encoder still folds across all four GPUs:

[4×H100 fastest]

[4×H100 fastest]4×H100 fastest

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 \
      --model-variant fl2va \
      --num-gpus 4 \
      --tp-size 2 \
      --ulysses-degree 2 \
      --encoder-parallel auto \
      --performance-mode speed \
      --port 30010
<
Pure Ulysses4 could not keep the full pipeline resident on 80 GB H100s. Use
`--tp-size 4 --ulysses-degree 1` when lower resident memory matters more
than the last few percent of latency. FSDP remains a verified capacity
option, but its per-block weight all-gathers do not make it the H100 speed
default:

[4×H100 FSDP capacity]

[4×H100 FSDP capacity]4×H100 FSDP capacity

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 \
      --model-variant fl2va \
      --num-gpus 4 \
      --ulysses-degree 4 \
      --encoder-parallel auto \
      --performance-mode speed \
      --use-fsdp-inference true \
      --port 30010
<
For a two-card RTX 5090 host, use TP2 and keep 20 DiT blocks resident.
Layerwise placement is lossless: it changes parameter placement and transfer
scheduling, not the BF16/FP32 denoising or VAE math. This is the fastest
measured 32 GB operating point:

[2×RTX 5090 fastest lossless]

[2×RTX 5090 fastest lossless]2×RTX 5090 fastest lossless

>bash
    sglang serve \
      --model-path MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 \
      --model-variant fl2va \
      --num-gpus 2 \
      --tp-size 2 \
      --ulysses-degree 1 \
      --encoder-parallel auto \
      --performance-mode memory \
      --layerwise-offload-components dit,text_encoder,vae \
      --dit-offload-prefetch-size 1 \
      --dit-layerwise-resident-layers 20 \
      --enable-torch-compile false \
      --port 30010
<
The DiT residency and prefetch knobs apply only to the repeatedly executed
DiT blocks. The text encoder and the video VAE decoder blocks use one-layer
prefetch with zero resident layers. The video VAE encoder stays resident
because its indexed down blocks cannot host executable layerwise hooks; the
roughly 577 MiB audio VAE also stays resident because offloading it only
adds transfer overhead. This exact recipe was validated on 2× RTX 5090 (32
GB each) and a 377 GiB host; use a 384 GiB-class machine. The latency and
memory comparison is collected in the benchmark section below.

For a single 24 GB consumer card (RTX 4090), stream the DiT and text encoder
and quantize DiT linear layers online with `convrot_int8`. Keep `vae` out of
`--layerwise-offload-components`: putting the VAE decoder in layerwise
offload re-streams about 9 GiB on each of 167 decode tiles. Default
attention stays `fa` (exact). Approximate backends are opt-in; see
|docs-sglang-diffusion-attention_backends-sage-then-sol-hybrid|. On the RTX
4090 (CC 8.9) `convrot_int8` runs on `comfy-kitchen`, so install it first
(`pip install comfy-kitchen`); on CC 9.0, 10.0, 12.0 and 12.1 it uses
SGLang's JIT-compiled ConvRot ops instead (built once per machine on first
use).

[1×RTX 4090 24GB]

[1×RTX 4090 24GB]1×RTX 4090 24GB

>bash
    sglang generate \
      --model-path MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 \
      --model-variant fl2va \
      --quantization convrot_int8 \
      --attention-backend fa \
      --performance-mode memory \
      --layerwise-offload-components dit,text_encoder \
      --dit-offload-prefetch-size 1 \
      --dit-layerwise-resident-layers 0 \
      --enable-torch-compile false \
      --prompt "A cat walking on a sunny beach, gentle waves." \
      --save-output
<
The same flags work on `sglang serve`. Drop `--quantization` for the BF16
baseline; everything else stays identical. GPU peak stays about 18 GB either
way because streaming offload is set by the offload buffers and VAE decode,
not the weight dtype.

To skip DiT steps on 4090 while keeping `fa`, add Spectrum to the generate
command above. Do not combine it with `quality="high"` or Cache-DiT. On this
card, 50-step BF16+FA (1008.6 s) drops to 285.3 s (3.53×) with exact
attention, up to 219.0 s (4.61×) with Sage→Sol hybrid + Spectrum. See the
4090 table in Benchmarks.

>bash
      --enable-spectrum \
      --spectrum-warmup-steps 11 \
      --spectrum-window-size 5 \
      --spectrum-flex-window 1.0
<
The first launch resolves every selected source through the normal Hub path.
If a repository requires authentication, export a Hugging Face token in the
server environment; no manual pre-download is required.

For MiniMax-H3, `--performance-mode speed` deliberately keeps the DiT eager.
The current `torch.compile` path changes the model's numerical output, so no
recommended lossless preset enables it implicitly. An explicit
`--enable-torch-compile true` remains available for controlled experiments,
but do not use it to generate consistency ground truth.

                 *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-one-server-for-both-partitions*
One server for both partitions

`--model-variant fl2va+ref2va` keeps the FL2VA and the Ref2VA transformer in
one server. The text encoder, both VAEs, the tokenizer and the processor are
the same files in the two partitions and load once, from `FL2VA/`; the
Ref2VA transformer loads beside the FL2VA one. Requests need no extra field:
a `ref2va` request runs on the Ref2VA transformer, `t2va` and `fl2va`
requests on the FL2VA one. Both partitions must declare the same sampling
contract in `model_index.json`; the server checks this at load.

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    sglang serve --model-path Pika-Labs/Pika-H3-Flex --model-variant fl2va+ref2va --port 30010
<
- Memory. Each GPU that holds the DiT holds both transformers (61.73 GiB of
  weights each) beside one text encoder and one pair of VAEs. The automatic
  memory policy places them at startup from the free memory of the
  least-free GPU:

  - from 181.7 GiB (the 120 GiB H3 keeps for one transformer and the rest of
    the pipeline, plus the second transformer), both stay resident, as on
    B300;
  - from 120 GiB, as on H200, both use component offload: the transformer a
    request runs moves onto the GPU and stays there, so only a request for
    the other partition's tasks moves weights, and it waits for the swap;
  - below 120 GiB the server does not start, and the error names the flags
    that place the transformers explicitly.

  Explicit placement (`--dit-cpu-offload`, `--component-residency`,
  `--layerwise-offload-components`) replaces this choice; with
  `--dit-cpu-offload true` every request moves its transformer onto the GPU
  and back. Validated on 2× B300 and 2× H200 (Ulysses 2, 4 s at a 480 short
  edge, MiniMax-H3 at 8 steps): on B300 a request after a switch denoises as
  fast as one without; on H200 the denoise stage took 7.7--8.1 s after a
  swap against 5.0--5.3 s without one, and 57.5 s and 46.6 s at the first
  swap to each transformer.

- Adapters. |diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-pika-h3-flex-six-step-adapters|
  applies each partition's bundled adapter to its own transformer.
  `--lora-path` applies an adapter to both; the
  |sglang-diffusion-api-openai_api-lora-management| addresses one with
  `target`: `transformer` (FL2VA) or `transformer_2` (Ref2VA).
- Weights. For `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3` the server also downloads `Ref2VA/` of
  the same revision. The Hub cache keeps one copy of the files the two
  partitions share, so this adds the Ref2VA transformer (61.7 GiB). Pika H3
  Flex materializes both. Component and weight overrides are Unverified with
  this variant.
- Limits. The startup warm-up request is `t2va`, so the first `ref2va`
  request also warms the Ref2VA transformer. Cache-DiT, `quality: "high"`
  and `--minimax-h3-adaln-cache-path` need a single-partition server.

ComfyUI integrated mode *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-comfyui-integrated-mode*

The |sglang-docs-sglang-diffusion-comfyui| can run H3 as a per-step DiT
backend. ComfyUI keeps CLIP, VAE, and the sampler loop. SGLang loads only
the transformer in ComfyUI integrated mode (the plugin sets `comfyui_mode`;
there is no CLI flag).

Copy `python/sglang/multimodal_gen/apps/ComfyUI_SGLDiffusion` into ComfyUI
`custom_nodes/`. Load a shipped graph and set `SGLDOptions.model_type` to
`minimax_h3`:

Graph                         Task                DiT filename
----------------------------  ------------------  ----------------------------
minimax_h3_t2v_sgld.json      T2V / I2VA / FL2VA  minimax_h3_fl2va_*.
                                                  safetensors
minimax_h3_r2v_sgld.json      Reference-to-video  minimax_h3_ref2va_*.
                                                  safetensors
minimax_h3_t2v_sgld_          Low-res then 3D ×2  fl2va DiT
upscaler.json

Pruned BF16 / FP8 files from Comfy-Org/MiniMax-H3
https://huggingface.co/Comfy-Org/MiniMax-H3/tree/main/diffusion_models are
the usual loader inputs. GGUF goes in
`SGLDOptions.transformer_weights_path`. On a 24 GB card put the 32B CLIP on
CPU and enable DiT layerwise offload; use `convrot_int8` or GGUF rather than
the full unpruned BF16 DiT.

                     *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-singularity-hybrid-weights*
Singularity hybrid weights

Singularity https://huggingface.co/WarmBloodAban/Minimax-h3_Singularity is
an FL/Ref fusion fine-tune, not a complete pipeline repository. Keep the
official model ID for the encoders and VAEs, and select one exact
transformer file:

[2×B300 hybrid checkpoint]

[2×B300 hybrid checkpoint]2×B300 hybrid checkpoint

>bash
    pip install comfy-kitchen

    WEIGHTS=WarmBloodAban/Minimax-h3_Singularity/Minimax-h3_Singularity_ref2va_Pruned_v1.3_int8.safetensors
    sglang serve --model-path MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 \
      --model-variant hybrid \
      --component-weights-paths.transformer "$WEIGHTS" \
      --num-gpus 2 --ulysses-degree 2 --performance-mode speed \
      --port 30000
<
For the full INT8 checkpoint, replace the filename with
`Minimax-h3_Singularity_ref2va_v1.3_int8.safetensors`. SGLang downloads the
selected file automatically. Do not add an online quantization override.
`hybrid` requires an explicit weight override; it does not convert the
official Ref2VA weights into a multimode model. Request `task` and
`conditions` retain their
|diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-4-generate-video-and-audio|, including
`conditions: []` for `t2va` and keyframes for `fl2va`.

Start with `quality: "lossless"` and the standard 50-point schedule. Here
`exact` disables additional request-time approximations; it does not undo
INT8 quantization, pruning, or fine-tuning. The author's recommended Ref2V
Turbo LoRA is a separate optional adapter, not an automatically inferred
4-step schedule. Reference fidelity can differ from the official model; see
the author's guidance
https://huggingface.co/WarmBloodAban/Minimax-h3_Singularity/discussions/1
before applying the |diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-5-lora-recipes|.

               *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-advanced-precomputed-adaln-cache*
Advanced: precomputed AdaLN cache

The model card https://huggingface.co/MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 notes that about
13B H3 parameters are AdaLN branches whose outputs can be precomputed for
inference. The public base checkpoint contains the original branches, not a
ready-to-use cache. SGLang therefore keeps the standard path as the default.

  Warning: ~

  This is an experimental deployment path. It is intentionally disabled
  unless you provide an explicitly generated cache; end-to-end numerical and
  peak-memory validation remains required before using it in production.

When an inference-only deployment has a fixed sampling schedule, build a
cache from the already materialized transformer directory on CUDA, then pass
it to the usual `sglang serve` command. This does not alter the denoising
formula: the cache stores the BF16 outputs of the original AdaLN linears.

[Command]

[Command]Command

>bash
    python -m sglang.multimodal_gen.tools.build_minimax_h3_adaln_cache \
      --transformer-path "$TRANSFORMER_PATH" \
      --model-variant fl2va \
      --mode t2va \
      --num-inference-steps 50 \
      --flow-shift 12 \
      --audio-flow-shift 3 \
      --output /models/minimax-h3-fl2va-adaln-50step.safetensors

    sglang serve \
      --model-path MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 \
      --model-variant fl2va \
      --minimax-h3-adaln-cache-path /models/minimax-h3-fl2va-adaln-50step.safetensors \
      --num-gpus 4 \
      --tp-size 2 \
      --ulysses-degree 2 \
      --port 30010
<
`$TRANSFORMER_PATH` is the `FL2VA/transformer` or `Ref2VA/transformer`
directory in the normal SGLang/Hugging Face snapshot; the builder never
downloads a second copy. A cache only covers the scheduler settings used to
create it, including its mode, step count, flow shifts, and condition noise
values. SGLang rejects a request outside that coverage instead of silently
changing conditioning. Cache mode supports the matching unquantized
checkpoint only.

*diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-advanced-online-adaln-rebuild-with-a-host-cache*
Advanced: online AdaLN rebuild with a host cache

`--minimax-h3-adaln-online true` needs no prebuilt artifact: the server
drops the 24.2 GiB of `adaln_proj` weights from the GPU and computes each
request's AdaLN outputs from the checkpoint on demand, bit-exact with the
resident-weight path. Because a rebuild pass streams the whole 24.2 GiB, the
first request of every new
`(task shape, num_inference_steps, flow_shift, audio_flow_shift)`
combination pays several seconds; after that the plans are served from a
64-slot GPU slab (LRU per plan) backed by a pinned host cache
(`--minimax-h3-adaln-host-cache-gb`, LRU per schedule, default 8 GB per
rank), so mixed-schedule serving does not re-read the checkpoint. Plan sets
that exceed the host budget are simply recomputed on their next occurrence.
Expert escape hatches live in environment variables:
`SGLANG_DIFFUSION_MINIMAX_H3_ADALN_GPU_PLANS` resizes the GPU slab (needed
only above 64 inference steps) and `SGLANG_DIFFUSION_MINIMAX_H3_ADALN_FP32`
computes the one-time projections in fp32 (experimental; not bit-comparable
to resident weights, validate end-to-end before production). LoRA adapters
that modify `adaln_proj` are rejected in both cache modes rather than
silently ignored.

Both cache modes hold values derived from `adaln_proj`, so a runtime weight
update is only accepted when the cache can follow it: online mode takes a
disk update whose target directory carries native `adaln_proj` safetensors,
and rejects everything else (tensor updates, and directories without those
tensors) before a single weight is written. A sidecar is built offline and
cannot be regenerated in the server, so weight updates are rejected
outright; rebuild the sidecar against the new weights and restart.

                *diffusion-MiniMax-MiniMax-H3-serve-minimax-h3-on-ascend-npus*
Serve MiniMax-H3 on Ascend NPUs

For Ascend NPU, follow the |sglang-ascend-npus-getting-started-installation|
before starting the server.

The Ascend commands below explicitly enable the Cache-DiT configuration used
for the reported performance measurements. Remove these `SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_*`
variables to use lossless denoising. See the Ascend NPU topology comparison
in the Benchmarks section for the measured eight- and four-NPU latency.

The measured latency configuration also passes `--dit-cpu-offload false` to
keep the transformer resident on the NPUs. Omit this flag when lower device
memory usage is more important than avoiding CPU-to-NPU transfer latency.

For an eight-NPU host, the validated topology is TP2 + SP4 with Laser
Attention. Use Ascend Flash Attention by replacing `laser_attn` with `fa`.

[8-NPU]

[8-NPU]8-NPU

>bash
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_ENABLED=true \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_FN=2 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_BN=1 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_WARMUP=4 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_RDT=0.4 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_MC=4 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_TAYLORSEER=true \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_TS_ORDER=2 \
    HCCL_BUFFSIZE=256 sglang serve \
      --model-path MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3 \
      --model-type diffusion \
      --model-variant fl2va \
      --dit-cpu-offload false \
      --num-gpus 8 \
      --tp-size 2 \
      --sp-degree 4 \
      --attention-backend laser_attn \
      --port 30088 \

    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_FN=2 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_BN=1 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_WARMUP=4 \
    SGLANG_CACHE_DIT_RDT=0.4 \
